---
title: "Sticky Situation: The Deep Read"
---

# Before You Start

This is the **second edition** of the deep read of *Tales of Being Limitless: Sticky Situation*. The first edition covered the draft through Section 4, Chapter 6 ("Curtain Call"). This one covers the revised manuscript you sent, through Section 4, Chapter 7 ("Saw Right Through You").

Here's how I did it. I reread the whole manuscript in order, from the Prologue to the last line of Chapter 7, with the first edition open beside it. Then I ran every passage I quoted last time (about 1,800 quotes) against the new text. About 280 no longer matched, and that's how I found most of your revisions: a rewritten Prologue, a renamed villain, a new top speed, a different eye color, new lines inside the book's biggest scenes. Then I went back through with targeted searches (every hand, every whistle, every "not friends," every exclamation point, every dialogue tag) and rebuilt the numbers.

Every chapter entry has been updated. Where you revised a chapter, its entry has a **What changed in this draft** note. I've been careful about one thing: if I can prove a line changed (because I quoted the old version), I say so. If I only noticed something on the reread and can't prove it's new, I say that instead, rather than taking credit for spotting a change that might have been there all along. Where I quote the book, the quotes are copied from your text.

The book isn't finished, and I've treated it that way. Chapter 7 is a cliffhanger, not an ending. Nothing here faults the book for leaving threads open. Where a thread is open, I've filed it under open threads, theories, or predictions.

**How this document is organized**

- **What Changed in This Draft.** The revision at a glance: what you fixed, what you added, what's still open, and how the scores moved.
- **The Verdict.** My overall opinion, a scorecard, and what the book is really about.
- **The Book by the Numbers.** Word counts, structure, pacing, and the timeline I reconstructed.
- **Chapter by Chapter.** All 54 chapters plus the Prologue. Each one gets what happens, what changed, the small details I noticed, what the chapter is doing, what works, what I'd push on, and a score out of 10. The new Chapter 7 gets the longest entry in Section 4.
- **Character Dossiers.** Every named character and some unnamed ones, with powers, arcs, key lines, relationships, concerns, and predictions.
- **Themes.** The ideas the book keeps returning to, including two new ones this draft made visible.
- **Motifs and Callbacks.** Recurring objects and images, and every setup and payoff I could find.
- **Continuity Ledger.** What holds up, what wobbles, what you fixed, and what's still unexplained.
- **Theories and Predictions.** Ranked by how confident I am, with the evidence for each, and an honest note on the ones I got right and wrong last time.
- **Craft Notes.** Prose, dialogue, POV, pacing, and specific line-level habits, with numbers.
- **Closing Verdict.** Best moments, best lines, and what I'd want next.

A note on scores: they're relative to this book. A 6 isn't a bad chapter. It's a chapter doing less than the book's best chapters do. I'm giving you real opinions, because you asked for honesty and because the book can take it.

# What Changed in This Draft

## The revision in one paragraph

You did a real revision pass, and it worked. The manuscript grew from about 104,700 to about 107,300 words, but only about 800 of the new words are inside old chapters (the rest is the new Chapter 7), and the Prologue actually got 300 words shorter. Almost every chapter from the Prologue through S3 Ch12 was touched. S2 Ch12, S2 Ch13, and everything from S3 Ch13 through S4 Ch6 are unchanged. The revisions cluster in exactly the right places: the Prologue, Abby's death, Orion's choice in the dome, the Elias fight, and the tournament's watchers. Most of the new sentences are short and quiet, which means the chapters now sound more like the Prologue, the best-written part of the book.

## The biggest changes

1. **Orion's choice is uglier and truer** (S2 Ch7). He can hear that Kage wants to help, "But a small, ugly part of Orion didn't care, and it already knew what it wanted to say." When he runs: "He had made his choice at three. Now he was making it again."
2. **The biomass amplifies "what's already there"** (S2 Ch7), instead of "your traits, especially the negative ones." That moves the moral weight back onto Kage. The cruelty isn't the monster's. It's his, louder.
3. **Kage's refusal to call for help now protects his secret in every direction** (S2 Ch6). Huoyan's contact is on his screen, and the whistle is under his hand, and he won't use either "with what Nerezza knew." "He told himself he didn't have another choice."
4. **Abby's death has a new last thought** (S2 Ch6): "For once, every number was her own, and Abby knew exactly what they added up to."
5. **Abby's brother has an image, and Abby has a rule** (S2 Ch5): "She sat by his bed every night with her hands folded to pray, when she could have laid them on him instead." "She had waited for her brother. She wasn't going to wait for a whistle."
6. **Thanatos is now Thanatos Augustus,** with orange eyes and no scar (S1 Ch12).
7. **Orion tops out at 250 mph,** not 300 (S2 Ch6).
8. **The disguise contacts are yellow,** Abby's eye color, not gray (S2 Ch10).
9. **Reina's hair is brown** (S1 Ch7).
10. **Elias's speech is long on purpose now** (S3 Ch11). Kage's calm stretches the time-stop clock, so Elias runs out of speech before the hold runs out of time.
11. **Elias felt the biomass** (S3 Ch12): "Something in you was pushing against my enhancement while you sat perfectly still."
12. **The tournament is full of watchers.** Gene's whiskers twitch when Anubis trades Kage's future, and she has a veto. Huoyan is searching for Abby on his phone the whole time. Zulfiya keeps her hair away from Kage. Ailani squints at Kage and then at Orion. Lucifer's tail sways "like it belonged to something much bigger than him."
13. **Lance's sword saws** (S3 Ch9, S3 Ch10), and the groove it cuts in the arena floor sets up the cast-saw mechanic Ailani beats in Chapter 7.

# The Verdict

## The short version

*Sticky Situation* is a superhero-school story that keeps turning into a story about guilt, being seen, and what it costs to be cheered. The premise looks familiar on purpose: one hero academy, a lazy prodigy, a rival speedster, a healer, a demon boy, a tournament. Then the book spends 100,000 words checking every piece of that setup for rot. The number-one hero is a merch salesman. The tournament is a TV product. The villains offer the mentorship the school never gave. The healer dies. The protagonist survives only because a monster rebuilt his body, and the rebuild is amplifying everything he was trying to hide, including the cruelty.

That's a strong spine. Underneath the jokes, the shipping, the cowboy slang, and the glue puns, the book knows what it's about, and it keeps coming back to that.

The engine is **Kage Ozul**, and he's the book's best decision. His power is adhesive. He calls himself Sticky Situation so that the people he saves will laugh. He's an orphan who "stopped unpacking his bag." His founding trauma is a hand that held on and then held nothing. A boy whose power is attachment, who can't attach, whose great failure was failing to hold on: that's a premise where the power, the wound, and the theme are the same thing. Most superhero fiction never gets there.

## What the book does best

- **Setups and payoffs.** This is the book's biggest strength. The gold glitter in Abby's backpack becomes the whistle. The anonymous maid-outfit photo becomes Inazuma. "Another speedster, huh?" becomes Inazuma again. "Eat breakfast" becomes "I remembered to eat breakfast!" The claymore Kage touches becomes Elias's suspicion, which becomes Lucifer's memory. "Lose my number" becomes "Keep my number." "THAT NAME IS TERRIBLE! PICK ANYTHING ELSE!" and "I'm not yelling that in a crisis" come back word for word with the speakers swapped. Severine's "come to me when you are certain you are significantly superior" comes back over a cup of tea. Lance's power card says he stops "even when his hilt and hands are fine," and forty pages later his hilt goes dark while his hands look fine. The book rewards rereading.
- **Fights built like puzzles.** Almost every fight is decided by understanding a rule, not by raw power. Lucifer pushes Severine's half-dome barrier across the ground. Geneki loses by breaking the floor and touching underneath it. Kage beats Elias because excretion counts as an involuntary process. Kage beats Abaddon by choking him instead of hurting him. Ailani beats Lance by noticing that his blade saws instead of slicing, and by breaking the floor under his feet. These are good fight scenes, and they're good because the powers have real costs and limits.
- **Moral mirrors.** Anubis and Thanatos are both death gods and both "mentors" who treat Kage as an asset. Huoyan and Frostbite, fire and ice, are the gruff ones who actually care. Lance is the villain's son who chose heroism, and Kage is the hero student drifting toward villainy. Lucifer and Kage each carry a monster inside them, and Chapter 7 reveals that Lance carries one too. The cast is built in pairs, and the pairs argue with each other.
- **Big emotional set pieces.** Abby's death. Orion's count-to-five in the shadow dome. Kage's "King Kage" speech. Lance's ants-and-frog story. Kage sticking his tongue out at Alex in the middle of being destroyed. Orion alone, raising the whistle. Ailani counting the beats of Lance's blade. These land, and they land because the book earned them earlier.

## What holds it back

- **The dialogue is louder than it needs to be.** There are 2,145 exclamation points in about 107,300 words, roughly one every 50 words. I split them up this time: 2,008 are inside dialogue, about 130 are in the power cards (which earn them), and only about seven are in ordinary narration. So the narration is disciplined. The problem is that ordinary conversation is shouted, and when everyone shouts, the important shouts get lost. Chapter 7, which is nearly silent, proves how much more a quiet scene can hit.
- **Beat repetition.** "Nodded" appears 83 times, "smile" in some form 198 times, "laugh" 127 times. The dialogue-tag punctuation, which was my biggest mechanical note last time (54 errors), is fixed: I found two left.
- **Emotions get named after they've already been shown.** The book often shows a feeling well and then states it in narration right afterward. Trusting the first version would make the strongest scenes hit harder.
- **The first round of the tournament is a deliberate anticlimax.** Seven of the eight first-round matches end in seconds, by forfeit, fake trip, or ring-out. The book knows this. Inazuma and Anubis both complain about it in-story, and the joke serves the theme of a spectacle that isn't delivering. But eight chapters of this is a risk, and it only works because the scenes between fights are carrying it.

## Scorecard

| Category | Score | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Concept and premise | 9 | One school, a lazy prodigy, and a power that is also the wound. Familiar on purpose, then taken apart. |
| Protagonist | 9.5 | Kage is funny, wounded, clever, petty, and getting worse in a way the book controls. |
| Supporting cast | 9 | Up from 8.5. Lance, Elias, and Huoyan are still standouts, and this draft deepened Gene, Zulfiya, Kelly, and Reina with one or two lines each. Some of the smart characters still sound alike. |
| Villains | 8.5 | Thanatos (now Augustus) and Nerezza are frightening in different ways. Frostbite is the most interesting character in the book that I can't fully read yet. |
| Worldbuilding | 8 | Powers have costs, telekinesis has categories, the UN mystery and the Security Accords hint at politics. The economics of heroism are the best part. |
| Plot and structure | 8.5 | Up from 8. Strong section design and an excellent midpoint. The tournament's first round still sags on purpose, but the watchers added in this draft give it a second plot running underneath. |
| Action | 9 | Up from 8.5. Rule-based and inventive. The Elias and Abaddon fights are highlights, and Ailani vs. Lance is the best-choreographed fight in the book. |
| Emotional impact | 9.5 | Up from 9. Abby's death and the dome choice were already the book's best scenes, and this draft made both of them better. |
| Dialogue | 7.5 | Funny and characterful, sometimes over-explained, sometimes too uniform among the articulate characters. Severine is fixed. The volume isn't. |
| Prose mechanics | 7 | Up from 6. The dialogue-tag errors and mixed quotation marks are gone. The exclamation and gesture habits remain. |
| Theme | 9.5 | Heroism as product versus heroism as comfort, and what it costs to be watched. Consistent and rich, and now with a second layer about who's doing the watching. |
| **Overall** | **8.8** | Up from 8.5. A genuinely good story with a strong voice, whose sentence-level polish is catching up to its structure. |

## What the book is really about

The question under every chapter: **Is a hero someone who saves people, or someone people watch?**

Anubis answers *watched*. He wants cameras in the dorms, sells the tournament on TV, loves Kage's story because "an orphaned failure turned number one hero just sells itself," and bets Kage's future on a deal with Lucifer for better ratings. Nerezza, the most sadistic villain, rewatches Kage's screams on her phone because "children screaming in pain is next level television." Put those two lines side by side and the book's thesis is clear: the hero establishment and the cruelest villain are consuming the same thing.

Kage's first answer is *saves*. His hero name exists so that a rescued person will laugh: "A name that can turn something horrifying into something funny." He acts on that philosophy exactly once, at his lowest point, when he sticks out his tongue at Alex in the stands while a ten-foot demon slams him into the floor. That's the purest heroism in the book.

But Kage wants to be watched too. He wants the crowd "forever." He bolts to be first through the stadium doors. And the biomass inside him amplifies "what's already there," so his need to be seen is getting louder alongside everything else. The tragedy the book is setting up is a boy whose heroism comes from comforting people and whose hunger comes from wanting to be looked at, with something inside him feeding the hunger.

This draft added a second layer to that question. The tournament is now full of watchers who aren't the crowd: Gene with her veto and her twitching whiskers, Huoyan searching his phone for a dead student, Elias feeling something push back inside a frozen boy, Zulfiya keeping her hair away from him, Ailani squinting at both Kage and Orion. And in Chapter 7, two fighters perform for Kage's attention, so the boy who wanted to be watched becomes the watcher. The book has stopped asking only "Who's watching Kage?" It's asking what each of them is looking for, and who will see him clearly first. The chapter title answers: Ailani, who "saw right through" Lance and is one win away from Kage.

That's a story worth finishing.


# The Book by the Numbers

## Size and shape

The manuscript runs about **107,300 words** (up from about 104,700) across a Prologue, four Sections, and 54 chapters (Section 4 opens with a "Chapter NULL").

| Section | Chapters | Words (last draft) | Words (this draft) | Average per chapter |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Prologue | 1 | ~1,050 | 751 | — |
| Section 1: Orion Vance | 15 | 24,181 | 23,959 | 1,597 |
| Section 2: Abby Neosame | 15 | 32,861 | 33,426 | 2,228 |
| Section 3: Elite Tournament | 16 | 36,585 | 37,343 | 2,334 |
| Section 4: Ailani Shoma (so far) | 8 | 10,046 (7 ch.) | 11,793 | 1,474 |

Section 1 got slightly shorter. Sections 2 and 3 got longer, by about 560 and 760 words, which is where the big scenes are. The new Chapter 7 is 1,747 words.

The chapters get longer as the stakes rise. The longest chapters in the book are exactly where you'd want them:

| Chapter | Words | Why it's long |
|---|---|---|
| S2 Ch7, Fractured Brought Whole Again | 4,108 | The dome, the 45-second game, the rebuild |
| S2 Ch6, Divine Intervention | 4,051 | Nerezza, Abby's death |
| S3 Ch11, Studying Is Important | 3,928 | Kage vs. Elias |
| S3 Ch16, Giving Into Yourself | 3,662 | Lucifer's mindscape, Kage vs. Abaddon |
| S3 Ch4, Forfeit Versus Failure | 3,124 | Zulfiya's forfeit, Elias vs. Cheyenne |
| S3 Ch2, Quick And Painful | 3,096 | Lucifer vs. Severine, Severine's meltdown, Lance |
| S2 Ch12, What's Hiding Underneath | 3,004 | Abaddon's first appearance |

S2 Ch7 is now the longest chapter in the book, passing S2 Ch6. That's the right order: the dome is where the moral weight is.

The shortest are breathers or pivots: S1 Ch3 "A Beautiful Past" (902 words), S2 Ch5 "Go Help Someone Else" (922), and S4 Ch5 "Running Off Those Calories" (1,105). The Prologue is now shorter than all of them, at 751. That's healthy pacing instinct. You're spending words where they matter.

## How the sections are built

Each section is named after a person, except the third, which is named after an event. My reading is that each section is named for whoever holds Kage in place during that stretch:

- **Orion Vance**: the rival who gives Kage a direction. Section 1 is about Kage finding something to push against.
- **Abby Neosame**: the supporter whose loss breaks him. Section 2 is about what happens when the support is gone.
- **Elite Tournament**: the crowd. Section 3 is about Kage being watched, and the watching becomes a character.
- **Ailani Shoma**: the person who knew him before he changed. Section 4 is about someone who can tell whether he's still himself.

There's another structural rhyme I really like. **The two sections named after girls both open with a first-year flashback built around that girl.** Section 2 opens with Abby approaching Lucifer in her second week of school. Section 4 opens (after the interlude) with Ailani meeting Kage over honey toast. Each flashback shows the girl at her most herself, right before the present tests what that self has become. If this is deliberate, keep it. If it isn't, make it deliberate.

The checks all pass, and this draft fixed the one I would have flagged next: the race is on a Saturday now, and S1 Ch7 opens "a couple days after his race with Orion on Saturday." Huoyan says "four months" in S1 Ch11, after "two months" plus "two more months." Huoyan's "only been what…six months?" in S3 Ch3 matches Inazuma's "past six months" in S3 Ch6. Nerezza's "it's only been a week since I've heard that noise" fits Monday night to Sunday. Kage's "only been a little over a week since we last fought" fits the Friday schoolyard fight to Sunday. Lance being told "you just met Kage yesterday!" fits, since they met on Saturday's walk. That's careful work.

The one soft spot is still there: in S2 Ch9, Huoyan says the two students have been "absent since Tuesday… it's been three days now." If that scene is Wednesday, the count is off by one. If it's Thursday, it's fine. A single word ("Thursday afternoon") would settle it.

## Cast size and the class roster

The Prologue says only **twenty** applicants get selected. Here's everyone I can place in Kage's year of the hero course:

Azucre (Rebento), Bandalore (Cheyenne), Cruz (Reina), Faisceau (Lance), Gardner (Geneki), Gardner (Rhatella), Lund (Alexander), Minkowski (Elias), Nasser (Youseff), Neant (Severine), Neosame (Abby), Ozul (Kage), Salyangozu (Volkan), Shifo (Zulfiya), Shoma (Ailani), Sokudo (Inazuma), Tulivalo (Lucifer), Vance (Orion).

That's **18**. Kelly Iasacht's son, who "didn't end up showing up," is probably the 19th. That leaves **one classmate we haven't met**, if the class is still twenty. That could be nothing. It could also be the most useful empty chair in the book.

The roster also confirms a lovely detail. Rhatella builds gear **in alphabetical order by last name** and says she's on "O." Every Rhatella-made item in the tournament belongs to someone earlier in the alphabet: Azucre's gumball machines, Cruz's heat pack, Faisceau's hilt (and, in Chapter 7, his spare), Minkowski's glasses. Nobody after "O" has her gear, including Lucifer (T) and Orion (V). That's continuity most writers wouldn't think to keep.


# Chapter by Chapter: Prologue and Section 1, "Orion Vance"

## Prologue

*751 words (down from about 1,050 in the last draft). Worldbuilding, then Kage's backstory, then the store.*

**What happens.** Everyone on Earth is born with an enhancement. Heroes get sponsors and cameras. Villains get "erased from the picture." The UN allows exactly one hero program, in one American high school, and keeps putting off expansion for reasons "no one's sure" of. Kage Ozul, an orphan from Japan, joins for the food and the locked door. He drifts through his first year. Early in his second year a villain attacks his favorite convenience store, and the person whose wrist he's holding is crushed under the awning. Since then he trains until his hands split.

**What changed in this draft.** You cut about 300 words, and nothing that mattered went with them. The thesis line is now plainer and better: "Ask most kids and they'll tell you it's the same game in different costumes" (it used to be "In many young minds, it's the same game…they just have different costumes"). The store paragraph makes the guilt specific: "Whoever they were, the awning had crushed them, and he hadn't been strong enough to hold it up." The next paragraph picks that up: he trains "like he's trying to lift the weight that should've fallen on him instead." The closing question changed from "someone just desperate not to lose somebody like that again" to "**just someone who can't stand the feeling of holding air again.**" That's a real upgrade, because the last line now ends on the book's central image instead of a summary of it.

**Details I caught.**

- "Some freak mutation dressed up as destiny." The first sentence of worldbuilding already doubts the premise.
- "Ask most kids and they'll tell you it's the same game in different costumes." This is the book's thesis in one line, planted on page one. Thanatos and Anubis are both coming.
- "Kids get scouted young, polished into smiling weapons with brand logos on their chests." This phrase comes back almost word for word in S1 Ch12, when Kage takes Thanatos's hand while thinking of heroes as "weapons who smiled because it was good for the branding."
- "Heroes are the product, and a product only stays valuable by staying rare." That's the best explanation the book offers for the UN's one-school rule: scarcity keeps the price up. Anubis's merch economy starts here.
- **The UN mystery.** "No one's sure why the United Nations only wants one program in one school, but it keeps its single polite little factory for 'hope.'" This is never explained. Later we learn Anubis needs UN approval "every single time" he buys a drone. Keep watching this.
- "For a kid in Japan named Kage Ozul, it was just a bed that didn't move every month."
- "He wears the air of someone who'd tape his own laziness and study the footage to get even lazier." It's a funny line, and "tape" is quietly an adhesive pun.
- "You don't hang posters if you're not sure how long the walls are yours." This is still the best single sentence in the Prologue.
- Thousands of applicants, **twenty** selected.
- "An enhancement he refused to explain." Why refuse? Adhesive isn't shameful. I think this is a deliberate seed. See Theories.
- "Says, 'Doesn't matter,' and moves on. **Back then, it didn't.**" A small time bomb.
- Teachers called it potential, "and they said it like a prayer."
- **"The windows went white and burst outward."** Read that again after you meet Frostbite. White windows bursting outward is what frost does to glass.
- "His fingers were holding air." This is the grip motif in its first form, and the whole book grows out of it.
- "Atonement with no finish line." That's the most precise description of Kage's motive in the book.
- "Adhering to every rule, more or less. **Lately, less.**" The adhesive pun survived the rewrite, and the new rhythm is funnier.

**What it's doing.** It does three jobs efficiently: the world's cynicism, Kage's poverty and detachment, and the wound. It ends on the book's central question, now asked in the book's central image.

**What works.** This is the most controlled prose in the manuscript, and the cut version is tighter still. The sentences are varied, the images are concrete, and the rhythm is literary. "You don't hang posters…" and "his fingers were holding air" do more character work in eight words than some chapters do in a page.

**What I'd push on.** The tonal gap between this Prologue and Chapter 1 is still wide. The Prologue reads like a literary novel. Chapter 1 reads like anime dialogue with exclamation-point power cards. The new ending helps, because "holding air" hands off directly to Chapter 1's punching bag ("All the alternate universes where his grip held"). I'd leave it there. The switch now reads as a camera cut.

**Score: 9.5/10.** Up half a point. Shorter, sharper, and it ends on the right image.

## 1.1 Nice To Meet You

*1,134 words. Training field. Present day, shortly after the store.*

**What happens.** Kage punches a bag hard enough to leave knuckle dents. Orion, a classmate he doesn't really know, tells him to take a breather and mentions the store gently. Abby arrives with water bottles from her dolphin backpack and heals Kage's split knuckles. Kage, delighted, proposes a no-holds-barred fight with Orion since Abby can heal them both. Orion agrees to "a spar? With rules? Maybe," and Abby counts it down.

**Details I caught.**

- The first line: "Kage Ozul used to come to the training field to hit on chicks, but now it was where he made his hands bleed." It gives you before, after, and the flaw in one sentence.
- "All the alternate universes where his grip held." The Prologue ends on holding air, and Chapter 1 opens on the grip.
- Kage: arctic blue eyes, scarlet hair "drooped in front of his face." The droop matters later. He cuts it off in S2 Ch10 because it was "lazy."
- He punches "like a boxer aiming for someone's vitals." **This exact phrase returns in S2 Ch13 for Orion,** after Orion thinks Kage is dead. Orion takes on Kage's grief-punching. That's one of the best callbacks in the book.
- Orion's gold: rings, loc rings, lip piercings, earrings, necklaces, bracelets. The loc rings become ammunition against Rebento in S4 Ch5.
- Orion already knows: "You were the one at that store, right?" He's kind about it: "happens to more heroes than you think."
- Abby: yellow eyes, a "twin-layered halo," nearly silent steps, a dolphin-shaped backpack.
- Abby's first words to Kage: "I'm Abby, and your hands?" And then: "**It's okay, Kage. Everything is going to be alright.**" She says these words again as she dies.
- The first power card: "The girl is Abby Neosame! Her enhancement is Eyes of Divinity!" The narration is present-tense and exclamatory, like a broadcast. "Some think she may actually have fallen from heaven!" (The last draft said "Some are worried," which was funnier and stranger. "Some think" is cleaner, but I'll miss the worry.)
- Abby's limits: the patient must be alive, it doesn't work on herself, and serious wounds need speed. Every one of those limits matters later.
- "Fights need a permit; I'm not breaking rules."
- Kage dodges Orion's question about his enhancement.

**What it's doing.** It introduces the first two members of the trio and the dynamic Kage will spend the book in: Orion grounds him, Abby heals him, and he asks for a fight.

**What works.** Kage's grab for the water bottle as a way to escape talking about the store is a sharp piece of characterization. His instinct is to fight instead of feel, and the chapter shows it.

**What I'd push on.** Orion's first speech is almost a counselor's script ("if you need a breather, take one. Helps more than people know"). It works for who he is, but a rougher edge here would make his later coldness ("We're not friends") feel like a choice rather than a different character.

**Score: 8/10.** Efficient setup, warm, and full of seeds.

## 1.2 Birth Of A Rivalry

*1,904 words. Training field, same scene.*

**What happens.** First spar. Kage opens with tape, then spins and sprays glue. Orion reads him and kicks him across the turf. Kage gets up, rockets forward on glue jets from his toenails, sticks Orion, and lands a gut punch and a big glue blast. Huoyan (Hellfeather) wants to stop it; Anubis says to let them finish. Orion tries to end it with Divine Relay. Kage anchors himself with hardened glue and takes the full hit, coughing blood at 105°F, then grabs Orion's leg and slams them both into the ground before passing out. Abby heals them. Anubis calls them "brilliant," and as he leaves he aims a finger gun at Kage's back. Huoyan issues warnings.

**What changed in this draft.** Three things. First, you fixed the line I flagged: the two men are now named on arrival ("Her homeroom teacher, Huoyan Niao, the hero Hellfeather. And beside him, the number one hero in the world"), so nobody has to sort out which is which. Second, the exchange after the glue hit gained a real beat of choreography: Orion "slipped the punch and snapped a jab back, but the glue on his shoulder dragged his arm half a beat slow. Kage ducked it on pure instinct and buried a fist in Orion's gut." That's the power working as a debuff, which is exactly how adhesive should fight. Third, Anubis's enhancement hint changed completely (see below).

**Details I caught.**

- Kage's card: lipids are fuel, adhesives come out through the **nails**, hardening draws **cold from his body**, and overuse causes fever. These are excellent costs because they're bodily and specific.
- Orion's card: gold and violet, comet trails, "up to two hundred miles per hour," overheating and joint strain. Named techniques: Aether Step and Divine Relay. (Zephyr Mode is no longer in the card. It's now defined in S1 Ch6, at the moment he uses it, which is the better place for it.)
- Kage: "**Another** speedster, huh? Ugh, I hate you fast types." Paid off by Inazuma in S3 Ch3.
- "My power is made to slow things down!" That's a thesis for his whole fighting style.
- Orion: "Speed isn't just running straight. It's perception and timing." This becomes a running line, used straight, mocked, and eventually turned back on Orion.
- Kage: "You thought the dude who messed up being a hero would be easy pickings, huh?!" He's projecting. Orion never said that.
- Kage's temperature goes from **102 to 105**, and later to 110 in S2 Ch2.
- "**This time, he made sure his grip held.**" The Prologue wound, healed in miniature, in his first real fight. It's beautiful.
- "You…insane idiot…you nearly had me."
- **Anubis calls Huoyan "Hon."** "C'mon, Hon, admit it." I flagged this on first read. It turns out to be one of the most important threads in the adult cast.
- **The finger gun.** "Anubis aimed a finger gun at Kage's back. Something sparked at the tip of his finger. Anubis took off his sunglasses, laughed quietly to himself, and left." The last draft only said his enhancement "seemed to activate." This version is much better. It's a gesture you can picture, it's aimed at Kage's *back*, and it gives the reader a clue (a spark) without naming anything. It also rhymes with Inazuma's "gun to your head" jab in S3 Ch6. Anubis's enhancement is still never named, so this is still a seed. Add it to the other pressure-and-weight hints listed under Theories.
- Huoyan: "That reflects on me!" His first worry is reputation. That's a fun irony, since he's the one who later attacks Anubis for caring about appearances.
- Abby finished her homework "this morning."

**What it's doing.** It establishes the rivalry as a fight between control (Orion) and stubbornness (Kage), and it puts both adult mentors on stage.

**What works.** The anchoring move is the best beat in Section 1. It's tactical, it's self-destructive, and it ties straight back to the store. The adult reactions do quick characterization: Huoyan is rules and reputation, Anubis is spectacle and "let them finish."

**What I'd push on.** My note from last time (which tall man is which) is fixed. The only thing left is small. The spar's last phase has Kage passing out "smiling through exhaustion" and Orion "gasp[ing] in pain" in back-to-back sentences, which is a lot of narration of feelings that the dialogue ("You…insane idiot…you nearly had me") already delivers. Trust the line.

**Score: 9/10.** The rivalry is born, and so is the motif.

## 1.3 A Beautiful Past

*902 words. Training room one, the next day.*

**What happens.** Kage, trying to impress Anubis, visits training room one for the first time and finds Ailani Shoma, his ex, on a treadmill. She tries to leave. He blocks the door, jokes, and hugs her from behind. A sigil flashes on her hand. She tells him she's done, mentions the eight women he's dated since, and he corrects her: nine. She threatens to switch classes and slams the door.

**Details I caught.**

- He's attracted to her before he recognizes her. That's a nice way to show the pull is physical before it's history.
- Ailani is "Latina, Black, and Japanese mixed."
- "For a second, he forgot about Anubis completely. **He could almost smell fresh-baked bread and honey.**" This is the honey toast from S4 Ch1, planted forty chapters early. The new lead-in sentence makes it better: for one second, the boy who just decided to live for Anubis's attention forgets Anubis exists.
- She walks away "like she'd rehearsed this over and over in her head." She's avoiding him because it still costs her something.
- "Call, DM, text, email, smoke signal, letter in a bottle…?"
- She hides a smile and wipes it away.
- He hugs her "like he could hold on to the version of them that was already gone." This is the grip motif applied to love.
- "Just like how we met, remember?" Paid off in S4 Ch1: they met in a "fight."
- **The sigil** appears and disappears. She's choosing not to use her power on him. "Kage's stomach clenched on reflex." His body knows that sigil. That's paid off two chapters later in the mirror room, where "Kage's stomach clenched the same way it had in training room one."
- When she pushes him off, "**Kage could feel the emptiness in his hands again.**" That's the Prologue's "holding air," now used for Ailani. The book's two great losses (the stranger and the girlfriend) are filed under the same sensation.
- "Actually…it was nine." It's a funny line and a self-destructive one.
- "Leaving him alone with the hum of machines and everything he hadn't fixed yet."

**What it's doing.** It adds romantic stakes and shows the old Kage through the eyes of someone who knew him.

**What works.** It's short and it hurts. The chapter title is ironic in a good way.

**What I'd push on.** Ailani's reasons ("you did nothing, didn't care about anyone… still flirting") are still all stated in one speech. A single specific memory, one moment he failed her, would make the breakup feel lived-in rather than summarized. S4 Ch1 gives us how they met, but nothing yet shows how they ended.

**Score: 8.5/10.** Up half a point on the reread, mostly for "the emptiness in his hands." Small chapter, big ache, and a forty-chapter plant.

## 1.4 Rhat Tech

*1,373 words. Rhatella's dorm.*

**What happens.** Orion, enjoying a slow walk, notices a door leaking blue light. Inside is a dorm that's "much, much bigger on the inside," like "five power plants mashed into one," run by an AI called C.O.R.E. that tries to take his blood. C.O.R.E. explains its three objectives and lets him up to the workshop, where he meets Rhatella Gardner: four foot eight, wired, goggled, rat-eared. She builds gear for classmates in alphabetical order and is stuck on "O" for Ozul. Orion declines gear, asks if she has friends, and they fist-bump into friendship.

**Details I caught.**

- Orion's "guilty pleasure" is walking slowly. For a speedster, that's a great detail.
- C.O.R.E. (Centralized Operational Response Engine): androgynous voice, cyan eyes on monitors.
- **The syringe.** It tries to draw Orion's blood; he slaps it away. In S1 Ch9 it succeeds with Kage. Rhatella's lab has a sample of Kage's blood from before the biomass. That could matter a lot.
- "You do not wish to beg my creator for advice or support gear? You are not here to scold her for her creations? That is…new." That tells you how people usually treat her.
- The third objective: "riskier actions" in extreme cases.
- "You appear to be a model student. **One verbal warning from Mr. Niao, issued yesterday. Not yet filed. Acceptable.**" Last time I flagged that C.O.R.E. called Orion spotless the day after Huoyan warned him. You fixed it, and the fix is better than a fix needs to be: C.O.R.E. now knows about the warning before the school's paperwork does. That tells you how deep Rhatella's system reaches.
- "I'm on 'O' right now, but GOD, this Ozul kid is driving me crazy! **As a conductor, trying to make an item for a human insulator is the WORST!**" Kage is a human insulator. That's an odd thing to be, and I think it matters. See Theories.
- Every gadget is stamped with a cartoon of her face. This pays off when Kage sees the stamp on Elias's glasses and hesitates before breaking them.
- "As long as I'm useful, right?" This is the saddest line in the chapter.
- "You're officially my longest conversation with a classmate…ever!"
- Orion: "I like doing things on my own. That's the best way to improve, right?" That's Orion's flaw, stated kindly.
- "I wouldn't recommend having 'kinda' friends."

**What it's doing.** It gives Orion his own subplot and one of the book's most quietly sad characters.

**What works.** Rhatella is introduced with real loneliness under the noise. The alphabetical-gear rule is a great device that the book honors all the way through.

**What I'd push on.** C.O.R.E.'s three-objectives speech is a lot of exposition delivered straight at Orion. It's in character for an AI, and the book makes a joke of it later when Kage cuts it off, but you could trim it here.

**Score: 8/10.** Great character introduction; a little speechy.

## 1.5 Stronger Than Strong

*1,665 words. Training room three.*

**What happens.** Kage, respecting Ailani's wishes, uses room three, slams "MAX SETTINGS," and gets dogpiled by thirty robonoids. Abby stops them by voice and heals him. A text from someone he hasn't answered in ages shows Kage in a maid outfit, and they bond over both having worked at maid cafés. Kage keeps resetting max mode, refusing healing, and explains why: he never wants to fail to save someone again, and he'll be a "jack of all trades" so no one needs other heroes. Abby, moved, whispers that she'll be his number one supporter.

**Details I caught.**

- "To him, that wasn't training; it was maintenance."
- Abby counts "exactly twenty-seven cuts and fourteen bruises."
- **"Kage saw something gold sparkle down at the bottom as she got the bottle out."** That's the whistle, paid off in S2 Ch2.
- **The maid photo** captioned "Remember this?" from "**someone he hadn't answered in ages.**" The sender is revealed as Inazuma in S3 Ch3. The new wording is a better clue than the old "someone he knew," because it pays off exactly: Inazuma says he "never got a reply." It also adds one more person to the list of people Kage leaves on read, which is worth noticing in a book where Thanatos and Frostbite leave *Kage* on read.
- Black-and-white outfit, long stockings, black heels.
- "I AM NOT CUTE!"
- Abby also worked at a maid café. That's a fun, slightly absurd world detail.
- Kage turned down working there again this year: "I gotta focus on getting stronger now!"
- "In a real fight I won't have time for you to heal me. **You may not even be there at all!**" That's dramatic irony. She will be there, and she'll die there.
- Kage's list: Orion, "that glasses wearer and that horned asshole **who keep trading the top spot in our class**" (Elias and Lucifer), Huoyan, Anubis. The last draft made Lucifer "always at the top." Now the two of them trade it, which matches S1 Ch7 and makes Elias a real rival rather than a sidekick.
- "**They say my power is for rescue, support, and detaining.**" That's an important line. Elias later calls it "some weak rescue thing."
- "**I can't fail to save someone a second time!**" The store, said out loud to the girl who will be the second time.
- "You won't even have a use for other heroes anymore! I'm gonna be able to handle it all on my own!" This is his flaw at its most naked.
- Abby: "She never would have guessed that someone could rock a maid outfit one year and play the hero the next."
- "Kage…I will be your number one supporter…"

**What it's doing.** It explains Kage's motive in his own words, and it gives Abby her purpose and her doom in the same chapter.

**What works.** The repetition structure (get mobbed, stand up, press the button) is a strong visual for obsession. Abby's reaction, admiring and appalled at once, is exactly right.

**What I'd push on.** The maid-photo interlude is charming, but it lands in the middle of the obsession sequence and lets the air out. Moving it to before the first "MAX" press, or after the vow, would keep the escalation clean.

**Score: 8.5/10.** It plants the promise that S2 Ch6 cashes in.

## 1.6 The Race

*2,228 words. Across the city, ~2 months later, a Saturday morning.*

**What happens.** Two months into nonstop training, on his usual Saturday morning run, Kage sees Orion near a ramen shop and ambushes him with tape. Orion dodges. Kage challenges him to a race across the city with a quarter-second head start. Kage launches off a two-story cement pillar, flies on glue jets, runs out of lipids, tape-swings like "Arachnid Guy," bounces off rubberized glue, and names himself: first "Sticky Man," then **Sticky Situation**. He tethers Orion with tape and gets dragged. Orion wins and tells him to eat breakfast.

**What changed in this draft.** The race is now on a Saturday, and S1 Ch7 now opens "a couple days after his race with Orion on Saturday," so the calendar is cleaner than before. The long Orion speech I flagged ("You might be fast, sure, but my speed isn't just running…") is gone, and the chase is faster for it. Zephyr Mode is now explained in the scene where he uses it: "Zephyr Mode was Orion using Celestial Velocity at its strongest. Energy almost leaked out of him, and his body seemed halfway to dissolving into specks of light." That's a better place to define it than the power card.

**Details I caught.**

- "Don't make me blow out a joint dodging your sneak attacks at eight in the morning."
- Orion calls Kage "**Speed-Limiter**." Kage calls Orion "**Mercury**." Orion doesn't get it yet. In S1 Ch15 he says, "I am Greek, Kage, not Roman."
- "C'mon, man, you've got a fucking cheat code…"
- Orion's eyes are **amber-gold**.
- The chant: "Get sticky, get sticky, get sticky…"
- **Cement** is new: "Kage revealed something new… cement." Orion's verdict mid-race carries the practice angle: "He practiced this. He actually practiced glue flight."
- "Your power gives you superhuman reflexes too? C'mon, man, you've got a fucking cheat code…" Orion's flustered answer ("It's careful training, timing, and control") is the first time he's defensive about effort. He doesn't want his work mistaken for luck.
- Orion: "Oh, you wanna use my line?" Kage stole "try and keep up."
- "Arachnid Guy or something." A legally distinct Spider-Man, which is funny.
- **Title drop**: "YOU'RE UNDER THE WATCHFUL EYE OF…STICKY SITUATION!!!"
- Orion: "Yeah, that's definitely getting shortened. I'm not yelling that in a crisis." And: "BRO, THAT NAME IS TERRIBLE! PICK ANYTHING ELSE!" **Kage throws both lines back at Orion in S3 Ch6** when Orion reveals "Hermes." That's the best callback in the book.
- "Name's cool though. Kinda fits you."
- "This is you turning into dragged luggage."
- "I never wanna train with you!" He meant it "for about five seconds."
- "**Also…eat breakfast.**" Paid off in S3 Ch11.
- "You improvise. I execute. That's our combo." The partnership formula, paid off at Nerezza and against Rebento.

**What it's doing.** It's pure joy: the book's lightest set piece, and the origin of the hero name and the partnership formula.

**What works.** The escalation of inventions (pillar, flight, swing, bounce, tether) is great. Each new trick comes from a limit he just hit. Kage's creativity is the point, and the chapter shows it instead of saying it.

**What I'd push on.** My note last time was that Orion talked too much mid-chase. That's fixed. What's left is small. The chapter switches between Kage's head and Orion's every few paragraphs. In a race that mostly helps, because it feels like a camera cutting between two runners. But some switches land mid-beat ("Orion looked up in awe" arrives one line after Kage's "Right now…he was moving faster than a speedster"). A blank line before each switch would make the cuts read as intended.

**Score: 9.5/10.** Up half a point. The trim made the book's most fun chapter faster, and it's still the source of three major callbacks.

## 1.7 Class In Session

*1,932 words. Huoyan's classroom, "a couple days after his race with Orion on Saturday."*

**What happens.** Abby heals Kage's bruise and kisses his cheek. Ailani ignores him. Rhatella and Orion arrive, and Kage and Orion announce "We're not friends!" in unison. Kage explains the rivalry. Lucifer, Elias, and Reina arrive. Volkan is late and gets fifty laps. After class Kage flirts with Reina; Lucifer punches and chokes him and calls him a "sticky fucking freak." Huoyan then scolds Kage and Orion for covering "HALF OF THE CITY" in glue, though Anubis texted him to go easy.

**Details I caught.**

- Abby "already know[s]" about the bruise. "Why do you think I sat next to you?" Her power scans automatically, which matters for Lucifer later.
- The first "**We're not friends!**" I count at least eight instances of this motif across the book.
- Rhatella hisses and gnaws on Orion's arm: "I'D KILL FOR THIS RELATIONSHIP DRAMA!"
- Kage on rivalry: "When me and Orion are in competition, we understand each other… He's seven inches taller than me for god's sake!… **He could die tomorrow and I wouldn't really grieve.**" Section 2 tests that sentence hard. Orion believes Kage died, and grieves.
- Abby: "Will I always have to pick one?"
- Reina: small, **brown hair** (this draft; the last one had it white), dark blue eyes, strong posture: "She stood tall for her size." Lucifer: tall, orange hair, light blue eyes, dark red horns and a tail. Elias: "maybe only an inch taller than" Reina, jet-black hair, glasses. Kage's narration repeats the color ("the small but mighty brown-haired one"), so the change is consistent within the book.
- The top of the class: Lucifer and Elias "traded the spot at the top of the class back and forth constantly. But Elias, at least, was fairly humble when he was at the top." That sets up the S3 Ch11 humiliation perfectly. The humble one is the one who makes a speech.
- Nicknames: "Lu," "Luci," "Min," "Cruz."
- Lucifer is "the top second-year hero student in the world." Since there's only one program, that's literally true. It's a funny side effect of the worldbuilding.
- **Abby's eyes track Lucifer "like a hawk."** Her smile slips. This is paid off in S2 Ch1.
- "You don't have many friends, do you?"
- The late boy: black hoodie, dark red eyes, "Mr. Salyangozu." He's named "Volkan" a page later. Kage forgets him entirely in S3 Ch7. That's a running joke you set up three sections early.
- "Monday through Friday, you're expected to be seated by the time I walk in."
- Lucifer: "Anubis was looking at me first… I'll have no problem burning you alive." He threatens Abby with the exact same words in her flashback.
- **"Sticky fucking freak."** Frostbite uses the identical phrase in S1 Ch13. See Theories.
- Orion distracts Huoyan so Lucifer's attack goes unseen. He doesn't do it on purpose, but he stands in the way.
- "I have been dying to crack the whip on you two."

**What it's doing.** It's the full-cast chapter. It sets the classroom map.

**What works.** You introduce a lot of people without it reading like a roll call, because each arrival has a reaction from Kage attached.

**What I'd push on.** The Reina flirt is still the one moment where Kage's behavior reads more as a plot device than a choice. Ailani told him his flirting cost him everything, and he does it again in the same classroom as her. (I said "two days later" last time. That was wrong: the treadmill scene is the day after the first spar, and this chapter is about two months after that. Two months makes the relapse more believable, not less.) It might be the point, since he's self-sabotaging, but one beat of interiority ("he knew Ailani was ten feet away and did it anyway") would make it a choice instead of a convenience. It matters, because this flirt costs him Ailani again in S2 Ch4, and in S2 Ch3 he tells her he's changed.

**Score: 8/10.** A busy chapter that knows where its weight is.

## 1.8 Perfectionist Meets Wildcard

*1,764 words. The training city, same day.*

**What happens.** Kage storms into the "city" training area, has a panic flashback at a burning building, then picks a fight with Severine Neant, who was sitting quietly. She repulses him through a window into a burning building, and his shirt catches. He makes concrete gauntlets, glue-dives, and shatters her barrier. She sidesteps and he eats the floor. She steps on his chest, praises his drive, and declines his request for a date.

**What changed in this draft.** You fixed Severine's voice, which was my main note. Every contraction I flagged is gone: "Goodness, you are naive." "God, you are a riot!" "Your determination, while very idiotic, is somewhat admirable." She now sounds like one person from start to finish, and the formality makes her two laughing fits funnier because they break something.

**Details I caught.**

- "How dare he look at me like that when he knows damn well we're not fighting today?!" Kage is angry at a glance.
- The PTSD beat: "That night, the broken windows, the awning's destruction. **A wrist in his grip, and then nothing but air.** He looked at his shaking hands. 'It's okay. This isn't then. I'm better now…'" That's quiet and earned.
- "Honestly, being annoyed was a lot easier than whatever he'd just been feeling." He picks a fight with Severine *because* he just had a panic attack. Anger is his anesthetic. That one sentence explains half his fights in the book.
- The second fire beat: "One of the flames caught his shirt, and for half a second, his hands started shaking again. Then he stopped, dropped, and rolled out of the building." It's a good detail, and a dark joke: the fire-safety drill is the one lesson he *did* absorb.
- Severine: 5'11", bluish-violet hair, purple clothes and eyes, monotone. She speaks in "dislikes": "disruptions," "incompetence," and later "foolishness."
- **Kage takes off his shoes and socks.** Toenail glue. It's a lovely practical detail that he repeats at Nerezza.
- First concrete gauntlets.
- The barrier shatters, and the fragments "glitter" in the firelight. For a second he's proud. Then she sidesteps.
- "**I strive for perfection above all else.**"
- She steps on his chest. **Kage does the same to Orion in S1 Ch15.** He learns his victory pose from the person who beat him.
- "Your determination, while very idiotic, is somewhat admirable. One day, in my quest for perfection, I hope to have your level of drive. **But drive does not win fights.**"
- "Do you wish for me to lie to you?"
- "**Come to me when you are certain you are significantly superior.**" Paid off in Chapter NULL.
- "Orion is priority number one."

**What it's doing.** It gives Kage his first clean loss and introduces the perfectionist foil.

**What works.** Severine is funny on the page. Her formal speech and laughing-at-him-until-she-cries are a distinctive combination. The panic beat gives the fight weight before it starts.

**What I'd push on.** The voice note is fixed. The remaining thing is the date request. "From this angle though, he found her quite pretty" is funny, but it's Kage's third flirt in six chapters (Ailani in Ch3, Reina an hour earlier, now Severine), and each one comes right when he's losing. The book wants that to be a pattern, and S2 Ch4 makes him pay for it. Make sure at least one character names the pattern out loud before then, so the reader reads it as a flaw rather than a running gag.

**Score: 8.5/10.** Up half a point for the voice fix and "being annoyed was a lot easier." A sharp foil with a clean payoff later.

## 1.9 Rhatella In A Novella

*1,342 words. Rhatella's lab, ~2 months later.*

**What happens.** After two more months of losses, Kage seeks out Rhatella. C.O.R.E. scans him and draws his blood. When he cuts off its speech, it kicks him down a shaft with a giant boot. Rhatella, reading a romance novel, panics because his gear isn't done. Kage explains his hero-name philosophy, and she's won over. Orion arrives for a hangout, and he and Kage fight over her time. Rhatella decides they're in love with each other.

**Details I caught.**

- Facial, retinal, and fingerprint scans, plus a successful blood draw. "Hey! You better not sell that to anybody!"
- He hears "whispers about Rhatella, the rat-eared girl who'd hissed at him in class." The hiss from S1 Ch7 is now how she's known.
- C.O.R.E.'s voice: "Somehow, the voice was even flatter than it had been with Orion." Later, "C.O.R.E. let out an audible sigh, A.I. or not." That's a great joke, and the new line "**Orion had gotten a nice elevator ride. Kage got a massive boot**" is funnier than the old version because it makes the contrast explicit.
- **Kage's hero-name speech**: "It's kinda like…a name of an action figure a kid could be playing with… Every time I save someone, I want to tell them my name and make them giggle… **A name that can turn something horrifying into something funny.**" This is the moral center of his heroism. Remember it when Anubis's office has exactly one decoration: an Anubis action figure.
- Kage can make cement now.
- Orion: "C.O.R.E. was acting weird for some reason."
- Rhatella's monologue: "my rizz factor's fuckin' updating… What did my ugly ass even do to have two hotties arguing over my attention?!… THEY NEED TO ELOPE IMMEDIATELY!" It's funny, and the self-insult ("my ugly ass") is quietly sad.
- Kage: "And there better not be any robot arms trying to probe me when I get out."
- Orion: "He's just…Kage."

**What it's doing.** It gives Kage's heroism its philosophy and makes Rhatella a comedy engine with a lonely core.

**What works.** The speech is great. It's the clearest moral statement Kage makes, and it gets enacted at the book's climax.

**What I'd push on.** The Kage-and-Orion squabble is fun, but it's the third time the "we don't like each other" bit has played the same way. A new angle (Orion being protective of Rhatella in a way that surprises Kage) would freshen it. The book does exactly that later, so this is a pacing note.

**Score: 8.5/10.** The hero-name speech carries it.

## 1.10 Will Without A Way

*1,417 words. The field, Thursday after school.*

**What happens.** Kage attends Huoyan's optional Thursday class, which nobody ever attends. Cheyenne Bandalore shows up because Huoyan wrote it on the board. Huoyan smiles, spreads his wings, and shows them telekinesis. Kage tries to float a glue ball. Cheyenne tells him to relax and he snaps at her. On his last attempt his hair stands on end, the ball floats for a millisecond, he coughs, and he collapses, still shaking and vibrating.

**What changed in this draft.** Two fixes, both responding to last time. The telekinesis rule is now stated cleanly: "Most people can do this, **unless their enhancement is a body alteration like my wings.** I'm one of the lucky ones. I have a body alteration and control of an enhancement as well. It's like I have two in one." The old wording was tangled enough that the rule was easy to misread. And the collapse now has warning signs: "his brain was still incredibly scattered, and **his body felt heavier than it should have**," then "Kage coughed." I asked for one physical warning, and you gave two.

**Details I caught.**

- "Huoyan was used to nobody ever showing up, and honestly, he enjoyed it."
- "Are you trying to catch up to Tulivalo?"
- "Yes, Mr. Niao. I am here to train." Kage being formal is a sign of how much he wants this.
- Cheyenne: unbuttoned blazer, loose red necktie, crimson-and-gold serape, a cowboy hat "she never took off," spurred boots, "the weary intensity of a desert hawk," a strawberry lollipop held like a cigar. It's a vivid entrance.
- The **jingle**: "keys or pocket change." Her power is never shown (see S3 Ch4). I suspect the jingle is a clue.
- The staff forced Huoyan to advertise. That's a small sign of the school's PR pressure.
- "He had no idea Huoyan had any personality outside of…dictator."
- Huoyan's card: Phoenix, flaming feathers, **weakness to water**.
- **Telekinesis lore**: "Most people can do this, unless their enhancement is a body alteration like my wings. I'm one of the lucky ones." There are two categories of enhancement, body alteration and control, and Huoyan has both.
- Cheyenne: "It's very possible your power can't do telekinesis… I'm pretty sure mine can't." So Cheyenne thinks her power is a body alteration. Kage's power (excretion through the nails) also sounds like one. So why can he do it at all? See Theories.
- "You don't wanna grab a bull's horns! Calm down, partner."
- "I'm not like you! I-…I didn't ask." He catches himself mid-cruelty. Remember that he catches himself here. Later, after the biomass, he stops catching himself.
- **His hair stands on end.** That's static, or something like it. It's the telekinesis tell for the rest of the book.
- "His body continued to shake and vibrate."
- Huoyan: "He should've just failed!… Something's off." "His strict teacher act cracked for a moment, and real worry slipped into his voice." (The last draft called it his "perfect icy exterior," which was an odd thing to say about a phoenix. "Strict teacher act" is better, and it also says the strictness is an act.)

**What it's doing.** It introduces Cheyenne, cracks Huoyan open, and plants the strongest clue that Kage's power is more than glue.

**What works.** The collapse is genuinely alarming because Huoyan is alarmed. If the strict teacher panics, the reader panics.

**What I'd push on.** The collapse note is fixed. One new question: "his body felt heavier than it should have." There's no biomass yet, so the heaviness is either four months of overtraining or the first sign that his power costs more than glue should. If it's the second, it's a great clue. If it's only fatigue, the word "heavier" will make careful readers think of Anubis, whose scenes keep using weight and pressure (see Theories). Decide which one you want readers to think of.

**Score: 9/10.** Up half a point. Quietly one of the most important chapters for the mystery of Kage's power.

## 1.11 Bow To Your King

*1,179 words. Nurse's office.*

**What happens.** Kage wakes up with Zulfiya Shifo's healing hair wrapped around his arm. Huoyan tells him he's been training nonstop for four months and needs rest. Kage rips out the IV and delivers the "King Kage" speech. Huoyan, for once, has nothing to say. Zulfiya, who hates egos, is charmed and calls him "your highness." He leaves. Zulfiya teases Huoyan about having a new favorite, and Huoyan says, "I haven't told Anubis everything," and shuts his mouth "a second too late."

**What changed in this draft.** Huoyan's slip is now staged as a slip ("His mouth snapped shut a second too late"), Zulfiya's honorific is in the correct order, and her tease now says "dethroned." On the reread I also paid closer attention to how the speech is built. It opens with the first year in Kage's own words: "**Last year, I stayed invisible on purpose. Didn't unpack, didn't try, didn't give anyone a reason to expect anything from me.**" That's the Prologue's "stopped unpacking his bag," confessed out loud. And it turns near the end: "And they will have to live with the fact that they almost talked me out of it. **But they're not the ones I'm doing this for.**" The spite is the fuel, and the soul up above is the reason. After the speech: "Huoyan opened his mouth to lecture him, but for once, nothing came out."

**Details I caught.**

- Zulfiya's card: Mending Mane, blue hair that heals, strands "like fingers," but the hair can be ripped and grows back slowly.
- "Four hours. **No, four hours and twenty minutes.**" Her precision is used as evidence in S3 Ch4.
- **The speech.** "Somebody paid for my invisibility with their life." "'Move him to general studies before he gets someone else killed.'" "Just the kid who froze. Just the kid who let someone die…and didn't have the decency to admit he wasn't cut out for this." "**People will call me King Kage one day. That's not arrogance, by the way. It's a spoiler.**" "I need that soul up above looking down and knowing, really knowing, that I became a real hero."
- The general-studies line matters more than it seems. Kage imagines people saying it. In S1 Ch14 he finds it in Anubis's handwriting.
- Cheyenne lifts her hat, sees his eyes "glistening with tears he wouldn't let fall," nods, and lowers it. That's a great piece of nonverbal characterization.
- Zulfiya "usually hated people with egos, but… It sounded like he believed everything he said." "As you wish, your highness." "Let the king be."
- "His crooked grin had snapped back into place like nothing had happened." Two seconds after tears, he's asking Cheyenne to fight. The speed of the recovery is the tell.
- She addresses Huoyan as "**Niao Xiansheng**," surname first and then the Chinese honorific for "teacher." The last draft had "Xiansheng Huoyan," which put the title before his given name. The new version is the correct order, and it's a nice cultural detail done right.
- "Is he your favorite?! **Has he dethroned Lucifer?!** Are you and Anubis fighting over which agency he'll join?!" "Dethroned" now feeds the royalty motif. Huoyan has an agency of his own (confirmed in S3 Ch1).
- "Teachers do not have favorites. I loathe all of my students equally. **And I haven't told Anubis everything.**" His mouth snapped shut a second too late." Zulfiya: "So you ARE keeping secrets about him!" The new staging makes the slip a slip. This is a major seed that's still unpaid.

**What it's doing.** It gives the book its most quoted speech and starts the royalty motif (king, prince, knight).

**What works.** The speech is strong because it's specific. He quotes the exact words he imagines people saying about him. That's insecurity turned into a weapon.

**What I'd push on.** The speech is still one block of about 450 words with no interruption. Huoyan's silence afterward helps. If you want one beat inside it, put it at "Somebody paid for my invisibility with their life." That's the line a teacher who taught him that year should flinch at.

**Score: 9.5/10.** Up half a point on the reread. The book's most famous speech has a turn in it, and the slip at the end is staged better.

## 1.12 Whatever It Takes

*1,756 words. An alley in the city, that night.*

**What happens.** Still exhausted, Kage runs off campus until his legs give out and screams at them. A well-dressed man in an alley introduces himself as **Thanatos Augustus**, the villain Shifting Horror. He tells Kage that animals that never stop running don't evolve, pauses, and adds "They die." He offers to train him "when no one is looking" in exchange for "a few favors." Kage thinks about heroes as branded weapons and shakes his hand. Thanatos holds the grip a beat too long.

**What changed in this draft.** This is the chapter with the biggest character-level revisions in Section 1.

- **His name.** He was Thanatos Shioya. Now he's **Thanatos Augustus**, and "Shioya" is gone from the whole manuscript. I like the change a lot. Augustus is the title Rome gave its first emperor, and it means something like "the revered one." Put it next to King Kage, the "Prince" nickname, Sir Lance, and Knight Volkan, and the villain who recruits Kage now has the most imperial name in the book. "Thanatos Augustus" reads as "Death, Exalted." That's exactly the man who says "My name is…" "as if granting a privilege."
- **His face.** The scar from face to neck and the thumb that rubbed it are gone, and his eyes are **orange** instead of purple. The orange is used consistently later ("The orange eyes and white tips in his hair made it obvious who this was" in S2 Ch6, "His orange eyes stayed on the spot where Nerezza had disappeared" in S2 Ch7). Losing the scar costs you a nervous habit, but it fits a man who can regrow his own tissue. A shapeshifter with a permanent scar would have needed an explanation.
- **His best line has a pause in it now.** "You're chasing strength the way a starving animal chases meat, and animals that never stop running don't evolve." Then a paragraph where his gaze traces Kage's "strained fibers, the overworked heart." Then: "**They die.**" The pause improves it. He looks Kage over and decides he means it.

**Details I caught.**

- **The handshake.** "He grabbed the hand and shook it tentatively. **Thanatos's grip closed around it firmly and held a beat too long.**" The book's motif is a grip that fails. Here it's a grip that holds too long, from the wrong person.

- **The villain card has no exclamation points.** "This man is the villain Shifting Horror. His enhancement is Bio-Forge." Hero cards are broadcasts. Villain cards are case files. That's one of the smartest stylistic decisions in the book. Keep it everywhere.
- Bio-Forge costs: cells in "overdrive," some inorganic materials "reject integration," and loss of focus brings "migraines, then blackouts." "He is never finished consuming."
- **Kage's hand twitches**, "a twitch in his hand he couldn't quite place." When he raises it to shake, "It was the same hand that had twitched earlier." Later his hand curls around nothing whenever Frostbite appears. Bodies remember.
- Thanatos: six feet, orange eyes, black hair fading to white at the ends, a black suit and shirt, a contrasting white tie that he fixes while he talks.
- "Boy, you seem like your body is going to break any moment now."
- Kage: "they make us call it an enhancement. I just call it a power."
- "I don't have some power that just cranks my muscle mass up thirty percent. I'm sticky, so I have to make up the difference everywhere else."
- "You speak like someone who's racing toward the top of a tower with no safety line."
- "**You're chasing strength the way a starving animal chases meat, and animals that never stop running don't evolve.**" … "**They die.**" That's a perfect villain line. It's true, which is why it works.
- "You let curiosity outweigh ambition, even for a moment."
- "Do you want to run past me or run with me?"
- "He always enjoyed watching how ambition bends before it breaks."
- "You're fighting a system that works against everything you naturally are."
- "I'd wager people have called you 'potential' for years. You don't want to stay 'potential' forever, yes?" The Prologue said teachers called it potential "like a prayer." Thanatos turns the prayer into an insult.
- "A few favors… nothing that conflicts with what you already want."
- "Weapons who smiled because it was good for the branding… **Was he being built to be a logo?**" This is the Prologue phrase coming home at the worst possible moment.
- "Kage's heart wasn't on fire, but it beat like he'd turned a page for himself."

**What it's doing.** It's the inciting incident of the book's real plot.

**What works.** Thanatos is persuasive because he's right about everything except what he wants. The chapter makes the handshake feel inevitable instead of stupid, which is hard to do.

**What I'd push on.** The paragraph of Thanatos reading Kage's body changed ("splints on a cracked bone" replaced "splints on a failing structure," which is better because it's a body), and it gained a new sentence: "A boy trying to outrun collapse by sprinting toward it." That's a good sentence, but the paragraph is now four images deep (micro-tension, tremor, splints, sprint). I'd keep the splints and the sprint and cut the first two.

**Score: 9.5/10.** Still the best villain introduction in the book, and the new name makes it better.

## 1.13 Partner, Not Pawn

*1,571 words. The same alley, the next night.*

**What happens.** Thanatos introduces Kage to Frostbite. Frostbite calls Kage a pawn. Kage objects, gets pinned to the wall with ice and punched, then glues Frostbite's mask. Frostbite tries to kill him and Thanatos takes the ice spike through his own shoulder. Thanatos says he won't save Kage again. The task: photograph Anubis's office. After Kage leaves, Thanatos admits Kage is a pawn, and Frostbite mentions "the guy in the chair."

**Details I caught.**

- "Down to the minute." Kage is punctual for villains in a way he never was for school.
- Thanatos pats his head. This is the first paternal gesture.
- "**Kage felt like there were other presences around.**" Nerezza recognizes him from this alley in S2 Ch6. And Anubis has a photo of this exact moment. So someone else was watching too.
- Frostbite: a glowing blue and black suit, a mask. "This one he recognized, but he couldn't quite put his finger on where. **His hand curled around nothing.**"
- Polar Shift card: frostbite "from the inside out," and "fire and explosions are the best way to take him down." Lucifer (fire) and Rebento (explosions) are both in the tournament, and Huoyan is a phoenix. That's three people in the cast who are built to beat him.
- "Horror…who is this?"
- "He's not random; I just met him yesterday." That's a great deadpan joke.
- The word "**pawn**" wakes Kage up. The motif comes back when Kelly tells Anubis, "now every kid left is a pawn putting on a show for you."
- "Sorry to ruin your 'smoldering aura farm.'"
- "**You sticky fucking freak**…I'll kill you!" This is Lucifer's exact phrase from S1 Ch7.
- "Sorry to give you the cold shoulder, Frost." A dad joke from the scariest man in the book.
- "I am not one of your teachers, or your savior. And I'm certainly no hero."
- Thanatos "spoke like a father talking to his child." That line sits right before "I will not be protecting you again." Fathers in this book are defined by when they stop protecting you (see Lance).
- The task: Khaled Nasser, Anubis.
- "Besides Frostbite's obvious abrasiveness, there was something about the man he really didn't like. He couldn't name it."
- Thanatos: "He is a pawn to us, but he can't know that." Frostbite: "extra info never hurts, but really, I just want to give him tasks that make him uncomfortable. Let's see how loyal he is."
- "I don't care what he…" Frostbite cuts himself off. "**I don't care what the guy in the chair does to me.**"

**What it's doing.** It expands the villain side into an organization with a boss, and plants Frostbite as the mystery inside the mystery.

**What works.** The power dynamics are clear and specific. Thanatos manipulates, Frostbite bullies, and Kage keeps insisting on a dignity nobody grants him. The final reveal gives the reader information Kage doesn't have, which creates tension for the next several chapters.

**What I'd push on.** Unchanged from last time. Thanatos explains the pawn plan very openly to Frostbite right after Kage leaves. It works, but a villain this careful might say less. Letting Frostbite infer it while Thanatos smiles would keep him scarier. One small thing in the new draft: Frostbite's line no longer mentions "the boss," so the first reference to the organization's leader is now the "guy in the chair" line at the very end. That's better. The reveal now lands once instead of twice.

**Score: 9/10.** The hand curling around nothing is the best quiet clue in the book.

## 1.14 Anubis-pheric Pressure

*1,360 words. Anubis's office, the next morning.*

**What happens.** Kage casts a glue key and breaks into Anubis's office. It's bare except for one Anubis action figure. He finds files labeled "Black Market," "Villain Encounters," and "Student Prospects," and photographs his class's pages, including notes on himself. The last photo is from last night: Kage pinned by Frostbite's ice. Anubis walks in. Kage lies that he came for advice. Anubis catches the lie about the collapse, gives a "systems" speech, and lets him go, then notices the open folder and that his door was unlocked.

**Details I caught.**

- The glue key cast is clever and in character.
- "The walls weren't painted." Huoyan later quotes Anubis: "I don't decorate my office because that would be a waste of money."
- **One Anubis action figure.** Kage wants a name like an action figure. Anubis owns one of himself.
- The notes:
  - Elias: "**Not showing signs of trauma.**" That's a huge seed, confirmed in S4 Ch3.
  - Kage asleep in class: "Why is he here? Should he be moved to general studies?" Kage's reaction is one of the saddest small paragraphs in the book: "He couldn't blame Anubis. Everyone else thought it too. **He just hadn't expected to read it in Anubis's handwriting.**" It's the line he imagined people saying in the King speech, and now he's holding it in ink.
  - Orion: "**Good fit for the Elite Program.**" That's the first mention of the Elite Program.
  - Kage at the spar: "Kage still needs a lot of work."
  - Robonoids: "**Self-destructive tendencies.**"
  - The race: "Exponential growth over a short period. Hero name stated to be: Sticky Situation."
  - Severine's barrier: "Advanced problem-solving skills. **Possible successor alongside Lucifer.**"
  - Frostbite: "**How did he survive the attack?** Kage is possibly stronger than originally thought." That's chilling. It also suggests the camera saw the ice but not Thanatos stepping in.
- "I'm trying to get on Anubis's payroll too," Kage half-jokes about the scouts. Then: "**he glanced at the phone in his own hand, and the joke stopped being funny.**" He's photographing Anubis's files for a villain. He *is* a scout, just for the other side. It's the best single sentence in the chapter.
- "Then he lowered his sunglasses an inch, and his voice turned flatter, like he was giving a mandatory assembly speech." "He waited." Two words, and they make the speech a test.
- "Kage, that's a lie."
- "The system is built this way for a reason."
- Alone: "I say the most teacher-y thing I've ever said in my life, and he just says yes?… **And not a single mention of Frostbite.**" Anubis knows, and he doesn't say anything.
- "I never leave this door unlocked."

**What it's doing.** It shows the hero side's surveillance and makes Anubis ambiguous. He knows a lot and says little.

**What works.** The folder is a great device. It lets the book summarize Section 1 from the institution's point of view, and every note is a little stab.

**What I'd push on.** It's convenient that every relevant photo is in one folder on the desk. It's fine for a heist, and the book acknowledges it ("He assumed he had just forgotten to put them away"). But that line might be read as a hint that Anubis left them out on purpose. If that's intended, push it a hair further. "He waited" already points that way: a man who waits to see whether a boy will argue is a man running a test.

**Score: 9/10.** The chapter where Anubis stops being simple.

## 1.15 You're My Problem

*2,432 words. The schoolyard, a few weeks later, a Friday.*

**What happens.** Kage has gone quiet. Thanatos hasn't replied. Huoyan orders Orion to talk to him. Orion finds Kage eating cheddar for lipids, and the conversation turns into shoving and an accusation that Orion "cock-blocked" his gear. Orion says it's Kage's fault he's weaker. Kage snaps, and for the first time his telekinesis works: six cement pillars rise and circle him while a seventh lifts him into the air. He seals the exits and calls Orion every epithet of Hermes. He lays glue mines, fakes tears, and wins with a telekinetic barrage of glue balls. He kicks Orion unconscious, cleans the glue off him with telekinesis, carries him to the nurse, and collapses at the door.

**Details I caught.**

- "Kage had gone quiet, **the same kind of quiet he'd worn all last year.** The difference was that he hadn't stopped training." Huoyan and Anubis read the silence as a relapse. The reader knows it's a secret.
- "Only telekinesis still wouldn't come, no matter how many glue balls he stared at." That sets up the payoff in this chapter cleanly.
- "Vance. Approach."
- Huoyan on Abby: "Not sure if it's a crush or whatever, but she will just affirm him. **I need someone who can actually crack him open a bit.**"
- "That boy is gonna die at this rate." **Huoyan caught himself and cleared his throat.** That's a teacher saying the true thing and then hiding it, which is Huoyan's whole personality.
- "Play a video game with him or something." Huoyan brings this up again in S2 Ch2.
- Orion: "Abby, Rhatella, hell, even Ailani asked me about him once. That freaked me out." This plants Orion's feelings for Ailani.
- Anubis got Orion out of "not one, but two" punishments.
- Orion breaks the hallway speed rule.
- The schoolyard has no roof, a lone gazebo, and doors on both sides.
- **Sharp cheddar** for lipids. "I really didn't feel like drinking straight up oil. I've done it before. That shit is nasty."
- "Go make out with Rhatty-whatever her name is." Orion: "It's not even like that between us."
- "You're not even top of the class."
- Orion: "It's not my fault you're weaker than me; it's yours. **You're the one who did nothing last year!**"
- "His hair started to flick up."
- "Kage was a bit surprised by his own display. **This was the first time he'd actually been able to do telekinesis right. He couldn't let his own shock show, though.**" The power works the first time he's angry and certain. S2 Ch12 explains why that matters.
- **The Hermes epithets**: Diaktoros (guide), Khrysorrhapis (golden wand), Angelos (messenger), Polygios, Kyllenios (of Mount Kyllene), and finally Hermes. Kage really knows his mythology. That fits the Prologue's "sharp enough to ace anything."
- Orion: "Ah, so that's why you called me Mercury before. **I am Greek, Kage, not Roman.**" It's ambiguous, in a good way. It could mean Orion has Greek heritage, or that he means he'd be the Greek god, not the Roman one.
- "This isn't a movie, asshole! You don't get to monologue! **If I was a villain, you'd already be dead!**" He says this two chapters after shaking a villain's hand.
- "Turns out we're falling, Angelos!"
- **Glue mines**: compressed balls that explode on contact. "Well, I guess it wouldn't be 'pyro,' but still!"
- "Awww, Polygios is all gunked up!"
- Orion's counter: "Perception and timing… you had let your guard down to brag about your little trick. **Thought we weren't allowed to monologue?**" Orion throws Kage's own rule back at him in the same fight. The book does this callback-inside-a-chapter move more and more as it goes.
- The tears: "This isn't fair! I deserve to win!… D-Don't you think I'm strong…?" Then one laugh. **Were the tears fake?** The book doesn't say, and I'd keep it that way. My read is that they were real and he weaponized them anyway.
