# Continuity Ledger

## What holds up (and is easy to miss)

These are things I checked specifically because they're easy to get wrong in a book this long. They're all consistent.

- **The calendar.** Two months, then two more, matches Huoyan's "four months." "Six months" from Huoyan and Inazuma match. Nerezza's "a week." Kage's "a little over a week." See the timeline in Part 2.
- **Kage's fevers**: 102, 105, then 110. They only go up.
- **Orion's speeds**: up to 200 mph officially, 250 when he breaks his limit for Abby (it was 300 in the last draft), 100 with a passenger. The 250 isn't a contradiction. It's the point, and the chapter repeats the number so the reader feels it: "Two hundred fifty miles per hour, and it hadn't mattered."
- **Thanatos's orange eyes** are the same in S1 Ch12, S2 Ch6, and S2 Ch7, and his new surname appears exactly once, when he introduces himself. There's no trace of the old name or the old scar anywhere. That's a clean revision.
- **Rhatella's alphabetical gear.** Every Rhatella item in the tournament belongs to a student before "O."
- **Kage's fire vulnerability.** Huoyan's fireball burns him before the biomass, and Lucifer's fire doesn't (or rather, it heals instantly) after. Huoyan notices.
- **Elias's power under a hold.** Vital processes continue, which is why Cheyenne and Kage can talk while frozen, and why Kage's excretion still works. The rule is introduced in the card and paid off in the fight.
- **Lance's blade.** The card says it "saws through almost anything rigid" and that flesh "moves with the blade." Against Volkan it "slowly sawed a long groove through the arena floor," and Lance explains that Volkan's rigid body is "exactly what my blade saws through." Against Ailani, the blade "was more like a cast saw" and can't instantly cut her crystals. Three chapters, one rule, and it's a real-world rule: cast saws oscillate, so they cut plaster and only vibrate skin.
- **Lance's limit.** The card says he stops "even when his hilt and hands are fine." In S4 Ch7: "Lance's hilt had no sword formed out of it, even though his hands looked fine." Then King Arthur. And "when fighting sometimes you end up as someone else" (S4 Ch3) is cited back.
- **Ailani's costs.** Her S4 Ch1 card promises crystallization "starting at the fingertips" and "a war hammer." In S4 Ch7 her fingertips go first, then her palms, and her last construct is a hammer.
- **Elias's clock.** "A still object barely spends a second of it," and living things can struggle. In S3 Ch11, Kage's refusal to struggle stretches the clock so long that Elias runs out of speech before it runs out of time. The rule from the card becomes a plot point.
- **The out-of-bounds rules.** Standing on a construct above the grass isn't out (Severine, S3 Ch2). Touching under the floor is out (Geneki, S3 Ch3). Ailani uses both in S4 Ch7, and Gene cites the second by name.
- **Ailani's sigil** appears every time she uses her power, including when she's holding back (S1 Ch3).
- **Kage's barefoot fighting** whenever he needs toenail glue (S1 Ch8, S2 Ch6, S2 Ch7).
- **Anubis's drone** works when the Frostbite photo is taken (S1 Ch13–14) and breaks the night Nerezza destroys "a strange camera" (S2 Ch6). That also explains why nobody saw Kage swing across the city with Lucifer (S2 Ch12).
- **Anubis knew Kage was alive.** He saw him on camera Thursday (S2 Ch15) and said nothing to Huoyan, which explains his confidence and his smirk in S2 Ch13–14.
- **The claymore** is moved, noticed, and finally explained, across three sections.
- **Lucifer's never-sleeping** explains Abby's diagnosis, his yawning, Elias sleeping over, and his strange energy after Abaddon was let out.
- **Kage's telekinesis** works when he's certain (S1 Ch15), fails when he isn't (S2 Ch3, S2 Ch12), and is never attempted in the Lucifer fight.
- **The tooth** never comes back, which matches Zulfiya's rule.
- **Ailani quoting Kage's exact S1 Ch3 promise** in S2 Ch4.
- **The race is on a Saturday** (S1 Ch6), and S1 Ch7 says "a couple days after his race with Orion on Saturday." The last draft's timeline (and my table) had it on a Friday.
- **Volkan** has red eyes and a hoodie in both S1 Ch7 and S3 Ch10.
- **"Hermes."** Zulfiya heard Kage use it in the nurse's office in S2 Ch2, which is why she understands in S2 Ch14.

## What wobbles

None of these are serious. They're the kind of thing a careful editor would flag.

1. **Kage's contacts.** They're yellow now (they were gray). In S2 Ch14, Ailani "stared deep into his blue eyes." He only wears them for villain work, so this isn't a real problem. One clause the first time he takes them out would still help, especially if the yellow-is-Abby's-color idea is deliberate.
2. **"Absent since Tuesday… three days now"** (S2 Ch9). That's right if the scene is Thursday, and one day off if it's Wednesday, the same day as S2 Ch8. Pin the day.
3. **Abaddon's pronouns.** He speaks in the third person ("ABADDON IS NOT THAT STUPID BOY!") throughout S2 Ch12, switches to "I" in Lucifer's mindscape, and mostly uses "I" in the S3 Ch16 fight, except for his entrance line. I can read it as deliberate: after Lucifer accepts that "we are one," Abaddon stops treating himself as separate. If that's the intent, it's a great detail, and one line acknowledging it would make it unmistakable. If it isn't, pick one.
4. **Fixed: Gene's reason for switching the match order.** Last time I flagged that she didn't answer Huoyan. Now she does, sideways: Anubis "bet the kid's future, not me. I just made him roll the dice before he gets paid." And her veto line shows what she gets out of it.
5. **How Lucifer knew Kage and Ailani were talking again** (S2 Ch4). They reconciled in a hidden room on a Friday, and by Monday Lucifer had told her about Reina, "one of the few times he ever spoke to me." Who told him? This might be a future reveal, but if it isn't, it's a hole.
6. **Fixed: C.O.R.E. and Orion's warning.** It now reads "One verbal warning from Mr. Niao, issued yesterday. Not yet filed. Acceptable."
7. **Fixed: Severine's formality.** Her contractions are gone ("Goodness, you are naive," "God, you are a riot!").
8. **"No one else knew Kage called Orion that"** (S2 Ch14). Abby and Zulfiya both heard it in S2 Ch2. The line is fine from Zulfiya's perspective, but the narration states it as a fact.
9. **The match naming.** Gene calls Round 2 "semi-finals," the half finals "finals," and the last match "grand finals." It's consistent, just unusual. A reader who knows tournaments might stumble. One line from Gene explaining her naming would turn it into a character quirk.
10. **Fixed: formatting.** The last draft mixed straight and curly quotation marks. This draft has none left. Every quote mark and apostrophe is straight.
11. **New, small: King Arthur's blindness.** Once Lance is blind, he still "threw his smaller hilt at Ailani's now-extended arm and caught his larger hilt before it hit the ground." The text says this happens "before he fully succumbed," so it's defensible, but it's a lot of precise hand-eye work for someone losing his sight. One sentence placing it just before the eyes go fully yellow would close the gap.
12. **New, small: Lance's card says "in his hand."** "Lance is capable of forming any sort of blade in his hand." S4 Ch7 reveals he doesn't need to hold the hilt. That's not a contradiction (the card never says *only*), and the twist is framed as something "everyone figured." I'd leave it. It's a good example of a card that's accurate and still lets the fighter surprise you.

## Open threads (not errors)

These are things the book has opened and hasn't closed yet. I'm listing them so none get lost.

- **Why does the UN allow only one hero program?** (Prologue)
- **Why did Kage refuse to explain his enhancement?** (Prologue)
- **What hasn't Huoyan told Anubis?** (S1 Ch11)
- **What is Anubis's enhancement?** (S1 Ch2)
- **Why "someday soon"?** Why does Anubis need a successor soon? (S2 Ch15)
- **What is Frostbite's "other half"?** (S2 Ch10)
- **Who is "the guy in the chair," and what is his interest in Kage?** (S1 Ch13)
- **Why can't Nerezza kill Kage?** "The only reason you're still alive is because-!" (S2 Ch6)
- **Where did Kage see Frostbite before?** (S1 Ch13, S2 Ch10)
- **What does Thanatos know about Kage's past?** (S2 Ch7)
- **What happened the last time Abaddon got out?** (S3 Ch16, S4 Ch3)
- **Whose claymore is it?**
- **What did Abby see in Elias?** ("But, what about Elias..?" S2 Ch5)
- **Kage's blood sample** in Rhatella's lab. (S1 Ch9)
- **Abby's death is unreported.** Orion plans to tell Huoyan "after the tournament." Huoyan is still searching.
- **Orion's choice in the dome.** Only Orion and the villains know.
- **Kage's villain ties.** Orion knows. Elias suspects. Anubis may suspect. Rebento suspects the healing.
- **The Elite Program.** Who gets in, and what is it really?
- **The Security Accords**, and the next assassination.
- **Thanatos vs. Frostbite**, and Nerezza's "serious talk."
- **Cheyenne's, Alexander's, Youseff's, and Anubis's powers.**
- **Kelly's son**, and the possible twentieth classmate.
- **Geneki's tail.**
- **Lance's King Arthur.** Answered in S4 Ch7. New questions: why did he let it take him against Ailani, when he held it back all tournament? Does Cheyenne know? Has it ever hurt anyone?
- **Lance and Kage's wager**: a bow or a hat.
- **Where is Abby's backpack?**
- **Rhatella's unfinished gear for Kage.**
- **The love triangle**: Kage, Ailani, Orion.
- **Reina's anger** at Lucifer and Elias.
- **Inazuma's racism**, which hasn't been answered.
- **Zulfiya's hair.** Why does she keep it away from Kage? (S3 Ch4)
- **Gene's veto.** Who will she let into the Elite Program, now that she's seen Kage regenerate?
- **Kelly's enhancement**, which he used on Severine offscreen.
- **Anubis's finger-gun spark.** (S1 Ch2)
- **Elias felt something inside Kage push against his hold.** What will he do with that? (S3 Ch12)
- **Ailani's hands.** She goes into the second-half final with them "still locked stiff at her sides."
- **The yellow contacts.** Does Frostbite know they're Abby's eye color?

# Theories and Predictions

I've ranked these by how confident I am, with the evidence for each. I'm not trying to guess your plan. I'm showing you what the text, as written, is signaling to a careful reader. If a theory below is wrong, the clues might be pointing somewhere you didn't intend.

## Strong (the text is clearly pointing here)

### 1. Frostbite was at the convenience store, and probably caused it.

- The Prologue: "the windows went white and burst outward." That's what ice does to glass.
- Kage's hand "curled around nothing" every time he meets Frostbite, and when Anubis says Frostbite's name ("His hand curled around nothing at his side, but Anubis didn't see"). That's the same hand that held the wrist. In S1 Ch8, his flashback is "A wrist in his grip, and then nothing but air," which makes the curl unmistakable.
- Kage "recognized" Frostbite but "couldn't quite put his finger on where," and assumes it was the news.
- Frostbite's sudden kindness after Kage flips him, and "the other half, Frostbite would reveal to Kage later."
- His apology: "I shouldn't be threatening your life," and "the half he was willing to say out loud."
- He bets on Kage and calls him "a…student of mine."

If this is your plan, it's beautifully seeded. If it isn't, readers will assume it. Nothing in this draft weakens it, and the Prologue's new line, "he hadn't been strong enough to hold it up," makes the store's weight even more personal.

### 2. Elias was present the last time Abaddon got out, as a child, and someone was hurt or killed.

- "Me and Elias have history."
- Anubis's file: "Not showing signs of trauma."
- The Hellspawn card: "even fewer have lived after seeing it."
- Abaddon: "Being out again after years."
- Elias runs to the bathroom shaking. "You know I never wanted to feel that feeling again."
- He sleeps in Lucifer's room to keep watch.
- A claymore engraved with *his* family name hangs in *Lucifer's* room, on holders "specifically forged" for it, and Elias insists it hang perfectly straight.

My guess is that the claymore belonged to an Elias family member who was there, and Lucifer keeps it as a memorial or a promise. One more piece in this draft: Elias is now the only person in the class who has felt *both* monsters up close. He felt Abaddon as a child and, in S3 Ch12, he felt "something in you… pushing against my enhancement" inside Kage. If anyone connects the two, it's him.

### 3. Kage's power is more than adhesive.

- He "refused to explain" it.
- He's a "human insulator."
- His hair stands on end with telekinesis, like static.
- He kept vibrating after he collapsed.
- He survives a 110°F fever: "maybe my DNA is made to handle that."
- Huoyan: "Simple telekinesis like that shouldn't cause even the most rookie student to collapse. Something's off." And "I haven't told Anubis everything."
- Anubis: "stronger than originally thought."
- He was kept in the top class despite failing.
- Telekinesis supposedly isn't available to "body alteration" powers, and his power is an excretion, "super similar to sweating," which sounds like one.
- New in this draft: during his first telekinesis attempt, "his body felt heavier than it should have," before the biomass existed.
- Also in this draft: when Zulfiya reads his temperature at 110°F, "he saw Abby's halo flicker. She'd stopped healing Orion and was staring right at him." Abby, who "can see through anyone," saw something.

I don't know what the real answer is, but the clues suggest Kage has a second layer, maybe a "two in one" like Huoyan.

### 4. Ailani and Kage will fight before this tournament ends.

- "I don't want to fight you… I'd be pretty mad at myself if I did end up hurting you." "If we fought, I'd kick your ass, Kage!"
- Their relationship started with her beating him in one shot.
- Section 4 is hers.
- "This is my time, and I've been dying to perform."
- **Update after S4 Ch7:** I said the likeliest route was that Ailani beats Lance, then Orion, and meets Kage in the grand final. She beat Lance. She's one win away. The new chapter's title, "Saw Right Through You," points at her too. I'm more confident than I was.

## Likely

### 5. Orion's choice in the dome will come out, probably through Nerezza.

Nerezza knows it, enjoys hurting both boys, and is about to be busy in the hero city again. But there's a new candidate: Ailani. She's been suspicious of Orion since S3 Ch4 ("For a guy with no plan, he sounded weirdly relieved about it"), she roomed with him to watch him, and now she has to fight him. If the truth comes out in the next match, it may come out because she pulls it out of him.

### 6. Anubis suspects Kage's villain connection and is letting it happen.

He says "compromised" out loud and then accepts Kage's cover story. He saw Kage enter Lucifer's dorm and said nothing. He has a photo of the Frostbite alley. "A hero who has secrets in his pocket is the best kind." He may be using Kage, consciously or not, to find the organization behind the Security Accords murders.

### 7. The boss has a personal interest in Kage.

Thanatos "needs" him. Nerezza "can't kill" him yet. Frostbite almost says "I don't care what he…." Thanatos talks about "a boy who's gone through what he has." I'd guess family. Kage is an orphan with no history, which is exactly the kind of character whose parents turn out to matter.

### 8. Rhatella will find something in Kage's blood.

She has a sample from before the biomass. She's the only person in the book who could analyze it. She also missed seeing his arm heal on TV by seconds, which keeps her in the dark until someone shows her. That kind of near-miss usually sets something up.

### 9. Lucifer goes to Huoyan's agency.

Anubis said it: "You'll officially be Huoyan's problem." Huoyan is a fire user who already wanted Lucifer. Lucifer needs someone who knows what living with fire is like.

## Possible

### 10. Abby isn't gone.

There's no body. She "burst completely into light." "Some think she may actually have fallen from heaven!" "Not all of Abby's abilities are known." The whistle was blown for days. The section named after her continues long after her death. And Kage now does his villain work behind yellow contacts, the color of her eyes.

My honest opinion: I'd be careful with this. Her death is the best chapter in the book. If she returns, the return has to cost something real, or it will undo the death. If she does come back, I think the whistle should be how.

### 11. Frostbite has a personal connection to Lucifer.

He uses Lucifer's insult, "sticky fucking freak." He lingers over the unconscious Lucifer and almost touches his face. He's ice and Lucifer is fire. Maybe a father? It's speculative, but the lingering is pointed.

### 12. ~~Lance's "limit" is fear of becoming his father.~~ Answered, and I was wrong.

I predicted the limit was a moral line, the point where winning would need a "dishonorable" trick his father taught him. S4 Ch7 shows it's literal: a state called King Arthur that blinds him and makes him "fight-starved." I'll keep one piece of the old theory as a smaller guess. A boy whose father trained him to fight dirty, and who has a second self that fights without planning, has probably wondered whether the two are connected.

### 13. Cheyenne's power has to do with what's in her pockets.

The jingle of "keys or pocket change." She put her hands in her pockets before her attack.

### 14. Youseff is stronger than he lets on.

Elias, who studied everyone, left him off the list. Anubis says his "heart's just not in it," not that his power is weak. Huoyan smirked at his fake trip.

### 15. Kelly's son is the missing classmate.

The roster has eighteen known students, the Prologue says twenty, and Kelly's son "didn't end up showing up." New in this draft: Huoyan, who teaches the class, tells Kelly "Good job not forcing your son to compete." Huoyan knows the boy, and knows he didn't want to fight.

### 16. Zulfiya knows, or fears, that something is wrong with Kage's body. (New)

Her hair is a diagnostic tool ("four hours and twenty minutes," "one hundred and ten degrees"). She healed him twice before the biomass. Now she keeps every strand away from him: "It stayed tucked behind her ear the whole walk, and not a single strand drifted his way." Her smile before the match "seemed off," and her first words were an apology. I think she forfeited at least partly to avoid touching him on camera, either because she suspects what she'd find, or because she's protecting him. Either way, she's one of the few people in the book who could confirm the biomass in a single touch.

### 17. Anubis's enhancement has to do with pressure or weight. (New)

The chapter that introduces him is "Anubis-pheric Pressure." When he's angry, "the air in the room grew heavier with every word." When he stares Kage down, it feels "so gravitational that Kage felt he might fall through the floor down to the earth's core." His namesake weighs hearts against a feather. The only direct glimpse is a spark at the tip of a finger gun. It could be compressed air, or something else entirely. But the weight imagery is consistent enough that I'd bet on it.

### 18. Gene's veto will decide something big. (New)

She built the program, she has a veto, she twitches whenever Anubis gets his way, and she hoped Kage would lose so she wouldn't have to decide. Now he's in the grand final and has regenerated on live TV. I think Gene's admission decisions become the next battleground between her and Anubis, and Kage, Lucifer, Zulfiya, and Severine are the pieces.

### 19. Lance will help Kage control the biomass. (New)

Lance is the only character who has a second self and has practiced stopping before it takes over. He's also the only person Kage "actually calls a friend." If Kage ever learns to hold the biomass back on purpose, I think he learns it from Lance.

## Predictions for the rest of the tournament

- ~~**Ailani beats Lance.**~~ **Called it.** Last time I wrote that her hard limit (hands locking up) would make for a good fight. It was the whole fight.
- **The half final** (Ailani vs. Orion) forces the love triangle into the open. She goes in with her hands "still locked stiff," which is a real handicap against a speedster. I think Orion holds back, Ailani notices (she's been reading his control since S4 Ch5), and the fight turns into an argument about what he isn't telling her. I'd give it to Ailani, narrowly.
- **The grand final** is Kage against whoever wins that. If it's Orion, the ban becomes a public question and Anubis has to choose between rules and ratings. If it's Ailani, Kage has to choose between "I don't want to hurt you" and "should a number one hero ever be number two?"
- **The wager.** I'd bet Kage loses the grand final and stands on the podium in Cheyenne's hat. "A lighter crown" was already a chapter title, and a humbled Kage would be the right ending for a section about someone who knew him before. But I'd also understand if you went the other way and made his win the worse outcome.
- **After the tournament**, Orion tells Huoyan about Abby, Thanatos returns, and the Security Accords plot brings the villains back to the hero city.


# Craft Notes

These are notes about the writing itself, as opposed to the story. They're the most fixable things in the book, and fixing them would make the biggest difference to how professional it reads. This draft already fixed the biggest one (dialogue-tag punctuation), and that shows.

## Voice and tone

The book has two voices. The Prologue is literary, with controlled sentences and concrete images. The chapters are bright, dialogue-heavy, and anime-flavored, with power cards, catchphrases, and all-caps shouting. Both are strong. The transition between them is abrupt, and the book could blend them more. The best chapters (S2 Ch6, S2 Ch7, S3 Ch16) do exactly that, mixing the chapter voice with the Prologue's control. This draft moved the whole book a step toward the Prologue. Most of the new sentences are short, concrete, and quiet: "In the picture, Kage's arm was a smear, but whole." "For once, every number was her own." "He had made his choice at three. Now he was making it again." That's the Prologue's voice, showing up inside the chapters. It's the single biggest stylistic improvement in the revision.

The tonal range is a strength. The book moves from Rhatella's shipping to Abby's death to Kelly's smelling salts without losing the reader. That's hard.

## The power cards

The power-card introductions are a genre device done well. Hero cards are exclamatory and present tense, like a broadcast ("The girl is Abby Neosame!"). Villain cards are flat and factual ("This man is the villain Shifting Horror."). The cards also make promises the fights keep: Ailani's war hammer and crystallizing fingertips, Lance's "decides that he needs to stop," Elias's clock that "only burns when the world pushes back." Cheyenne's card is interrupted mid-sentence by the fight. Ailani's card is written in its own time period ("The freshman class's Ailani Shoma!"). Keep this device everywhere, keep the villain contrast, and keep holding cards back for characters whose powers are a mystery (Anubis, Youseff, Cheyenne).

## Exclamation points and capitals

This draft has **2,145 exclamation points** in about 107,300 words (the last draft had 2,119), still roughly one every 50 words. There are 674 fully capitalized words of four letters or more (664 last time).

I split them up this time, and the result changes my advice. **2,008 of the 2,145 are inside dialogue.** About 130 are in power cards. Only about seven are in ordinary narration, and two of those are the arena bell ("BONG!"). So the narration is already disciplined. The volume problem is entirely in what characters say.

The power cards earn their exclamation points. So do Gene's announcements, Abaddon, and real screaming. But a lot of ordinary conversation also ends in "!", and when everyone is loud, the loud moments lose their force. S4 Ch7 is the proof: it's the quietest fight in the book, with almost no dialogue, and it reads as the most intense.

**Suggestion:** In dialogue, keep exclamation points for shouting and real surprise. A good test is whether the character would actually be raising their voice. Then read the Abaddon fight again, and notice how much louder his capitals feel.

## Dialogue tag punctuation

**Fixed.** Last time I counted about **54 places** where a dialogue tag followed a period instead of a comma ("No, you won't." Anubis answered quickly). This draft has 132 correctly comma-tagged lines, and I found only **two** errors left, both in S4 Ch3, one of the chapters you didn't touch:

> "I trust Kage." Lance said to silence the murmuring room.

> "We hate him and don't trust him, but we're not gonna kick him out with some stupid popularity contest." Elias said to Rebento bluntly.

Both should be commas ("I trust Kage," Lance said…). There's also one that looks like an error and isn't: "Kelly." Anubis snapped. That's right as written, because he's snapping his fingers, which Kelly's reply ("Don't snap at me like I'm your fucking dog") confirms. Nice.

## Repeated beats

Some gestures carry a lot of weight in the book:

| Word | Last draft | This draft |
|---|---|---|
| nodded | 82 | 83 |
| chuckle (all forms) | 45 | 45 |
| sigh (all forms) | 48 | 51 |
| smirk (all forms) | 22 | 24 |
| grin (all forms) | 27 | 32 |
| giggle (all forms) | 20 | 18 |
| facepalm (all forms) | 12 | 9 |
| sucked his/her/their teeth | 10 | 10 |
| laugh / laughed / laughing | not counted | 127 |
| smile / smiled / smiling | not counted | 198 |

These barely moved. The manuscript grew by about 2,550 words (1,747 of them in the new chapter), and the gestures came along at the same rate. The two I didn't count last time are the big ones: 198 smiles and 127 laughs. Some of that is the point (Kage's grin is a plot device, and Lance's "your smile is pretty comforting" depends on it). But when every character smiles at every line, the reader stops seeing the smile that matters. Try cutting half the nods and a third of the smiles, and replace some with nothing at all. Most dialogue doesn't need a gesture.

## Telling after showing

The book often shows a feeling and then names it immediately. My example last time was in S2 Ch14, "The tension in the room drained from the hug, and everyone relaxed." That line is revised now ("but once again, everyone was chill except Ailani"), and it works better because it turns into a setup. The habit is still there elsewhere. "Could tell" appears often as a way of narrating what one character reads in another ("Frostbite could tell how emotional he was getting," "Kelly could tell Huoyan was desperate"), and "clearly" or "obviously" appears 23 times. When the showing is good (and it usually is), trust it. Cutting the naming sentence will usually make the moment land harder.

## Point of view

The book uses a roaming third-person narrator that moves between heads in a scene, sometimes within a paragraph. That's a legitimate choice, and it's how the book can show the hero lounge reacting to a fight in the same scene. But sometimes the head-hopping is fast enough to blur whose scene it is (S3 Ch4's Orion–Ailani conversation, for example). The newest example is S4 Ch7. The fight is in Ailani's head, and it's excellent there, and then it jumps into Lance's for one sentence ("Lance felt like he'd coughed up a lung") and for the King Arthur explanation. One technique: give each scene a "home" character, and only leave that head for a clear reason.

## Speeches

The book's characters give long speeches: Kage's King speech, Thanatos's offer, Elias's humiliation of Kage, Anubis's succession pitch, Abaddon in the mindscape, Lance's backstory. Most of them are excellent. But several run a third longer than they need to. The best ones (Lance's ants and frog) work because they're specific and short. Try cutting each long speech by a third, and keep every line that's specific. One exception now: Elias's speech in S3 Ch11. This draft made its length part of the fight (Kage's calm stretches the hold, so Elias has to keep talking), so it should stay long.

## Dialogue voices

The comic and loud characters (Kage, Rhatella, Rebento, Inazuma, Cheyenne, Gene, Abaddon) have distinct voices. The articulate characters (Orion, Lance, Elias, sometimes Huoyan and Anubis) can sound alike: full sentences, careful logic, formal diction. Give each a verbal habit. Elias already has one (he's literal and precise). Lance could be more old-fashioned, as Kage teases. Orion could be shorter and drier. Huoyan has ESL grammar, which Anubis corrects; use it occasionally in his dialogue. Severine is now a model for this: once her contractions were removed, she became instantly recognizable on the page.

## Chapter titles

They're good: puns ("Rhatella In A Novella," "Anubis-pheric Pressure," "Rhat Tech"), quiet ironies ("A Beautiful Past," "Honor Is For Suckers"), and structural jokes ("Chapter NULL"). A few could be sharper. "Honor Is For Suckers" could point more at Reina. "Which Kage Is Best?" now delivers, because Inazuma asks the question out loud. And the new one, "Saw Right Through You," might be the best title in the book. It's a pun on Lance's blade, a description of how Ailani wins, an irony about Lance going blind, and a warning about what Ailani is going to do to Kage. Overall, they're a strength.

## Pacing

Sections 1 and 2 are well paced. Section 3's first round is deliberately anticlimactic, and the book uses the gaps between matches for character work, which mostly succeeds. If you want to tighten it, cut the hero-lounge banter in S3 Ch1 and S3 Ch3 by about a quarter. Section 4 is moving well, and S4 Ch7 shows the book can now do a full fight with almost no talking. That's a tool worth using again in the grand final.

# Closing Verdict

## The ten best moments

1. **Abby's death** (S2 Ch6). "It's okay, Kage…everything is going to be alright." And now: "For once, every number was her own."
2. **The count to five** (S2 Ch7). Orion chooses at "three," and "a small, ugly part of Orion didn't care."
3. **The tongue** (S3 Ch16). Kage sticks his tongue out at Alex while being destroyed, and Alex laughs.
4. **Orion raises the whistle** (S3 Ch16). "The only hand raised up high for Kage in that lobby."
5. **"I remembered to eat breakfast!"** (S3 Ch11).
6. **The terrible-name callback** (S3 Ch6). "DUDE, THAT HERO NAME IS TERRIBLE!"
7. **The anchoring** (S1 Ch2). "This time, he made sure his grip held."
8. **The ceiling** (S2 Ch11). Kage watches his own mourners from above.
9. **The ants and the frog** (S3 Ch13). Lance's origin story.
10. **"Two counts to her one"** (S4 Ch7). The dancer hears the tempo of Lance's blade and wins the exchange.

Honorable mentions, most of them new in this draft: the photo on Ailani's dresser, "a smear, but whole" (S2 Ch8). "She wasn't going to wait for a whistle" (S2 Ch5). Kage typing and deleting "sorry" (S2 Ch4). The vote (S4 Ch3), which was on this list last time and only lost its spot to Ailani.

## The best lines

- "You don't hang posters if you're not sure how long the walls are yours."
- "When the dust cleared, his fingers were holding air."
- "A name that can turn something horrifying into something funny."
- "People will call me King Kage one day. That's not arrogance, by the way. It's a spoiler."
- "Animals that never stop running don't evolve." … "They die."
- "I'm happy to not be your friend, Lucifer."
- "She sat by his bed every night with her hands folded to pray, when she could have laid them on him instead."
- "She had waited for her brother. She wasn't going to wait for a whistle."
- "For once, every number was her own, and Abby knew exactly what they added up to."
- "He had made his choice at three. Now he was making it again."
- "In the picture, Kage's arm was a smear, but whole."
- "Angels can't befriend demons."
- "Monsters don't fear other monsters. They recognize each other."
- "You can't break…what's already broken!"
- "Oh, the straight man doesn't get to be funny too? You're putting me in slots, Kage."
- "I try to hold people to the standard I didn't hold to myself."
- "Children screaming in pain is next level television."
- "Orion's fist was the only hand raised up high for Kage in that lobby."
- "He just hadn't expected to read it in Anubis's handwriting."
- "Nobody had ever punched a wall over him."
- "Two counts to her one, and Ailani had been counting beats her whole life."

## What I'd want next

1. ~~**Ailani's fight.** She's earned it.~~ Delivered in S4 Ch7, and it was worth the wait.
2. **Orion alone with his secret.** He still hasn't had an interior chapter since Kage came back, and now he has to fight Ailani. That match is the place.
3. **Frostbite's "other half."** The book promised it.
4. **Thanatos's return,** and the cost to Kage of what Frostbite allowed.
5. **Somebody says "Where's Abby?" out loud,** in front of everyone.
6. **Kage almost confessing to Lance,** and stopping.
7. **A cost Kage can't regenerate.** He's lost a tooth. Next, it should be something in a relationship.
8. **Cheyenne's face when Lance's eyes go yellow.** One line. She knows what King Arthur is.
9. **Someone notices the yellow contacts.** If that color is Abby's on purpose, let it land.

## Final word

This book has a real heart, a strong spine, and an unusually careful memory. The setups and payoffs alone show a writer who's thinking about the whole book while writing each page.

This draft also shows something I couldn't see last time: you can revise. You fixed the dialogue tags almost completely, made Severine's voice consistent, cleaned up the quotation marks, pinned down the calendar, answered most of the continuity notes, and added a frame to the Abby flashback. More important, you went back into the best chapters and made them better without making them longer. Orion's choice is uglier and truer. Abby's death has a new last thought. Kage's refusal to call for help now protects his secret in every direction. Thanatos has a better name. And the new lines sound like the Prologue, which is the most controlled writing in the book. What's left at the sentence level is volume (exclamation points in dialogue, smiles and nods), and that's the easiest kind of work to do once the story is this solid.

It's a story about a boy who wanted people to laugh when he saved them, and who is being slowly turned into something that wants them to watch him win. In this draft it's also a story about the people watching him, and about one girl who has started to see right through him. The book knows that's a tragedy, and it's telling it well. I want to read what happens next.

**Overall: 8.8/10** (up from 8.5).
